Every answer comes from the same research as the review it links to — the same prices, read on the vendor’s own page and dated. Nothing here is written to fill a page: if we could not verify something, the answer says so.

1Password

Is 1Password worth it?

1Password is genuinely best for teams that want the most polished, secure password manager with solid admin controls and integrations. Skip it if you're an individual on a budget — a free or cheaper manager covers the basics of secure storage.

How much does 1Password cost?

Teams Starter Pack about $24.95/mo flat for 10 members (billed annually, $29.95 month-to-month), with up to 10 further seats at per-member pricing; Business about $8.99/user/mo (billed annually, $10.99 month-to-month), plus custom Enterprise. Personal plans Individual $3.99/mo and Families $5.99/mo on annual billing, or $4.99 and $7.99 month-to-month; the $2.99 and $4.49 shown on the cards are a 25% discount for the first 12 months, new customers only. No free tier; 14-day free trial (re-verified 2026-09-11). Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with 1Password?

It's the polished, category-leading pick, and it's priced like it — free and cheaper managers exist, so you're paying for the UX, ecosystem and business controls, not raw functionality. For a business, that's usually worth it.

accessiBe

Is accessiBe worth it?

accessiBe is genuinely best for small and medium sites on ordinary CMS templates whose owners want a visible, cheap first step — contrast, text sizing, keyboard hints and an accessibility statement — and who understand they are buying an aid rather than conformance. The visit-based tiers are honest for that use: at 5,000 visits a month $490 a year is real money but not a project budget, and the Scale tier is the only one that includes any human testing at all. Skip it if you need actual WCAG conformance, are responding to a demand letter, or run a complex web app with custom components — in all three the money belongs in an audit and code fixes, and an overlay will not stand in for either. Skip it too if you are shopping on the promise of automatic compliance. That promise is what the FTC ordered the company to stop making without evidence, so treat any version of it you still see as a reason to look harder, not to buy.

How much does accessiBe cost?

(2026-09-10, read on their own pricing page) Four tiers for accessWidget, priced per monthly website visits, with both a monthly and a yearly rate on every tier: Micro $59/month or $490/year up to 5,000 visits a month, Growth $179/month or $1,490/year up to 30,000, Scale $479/month or $3,990/year up to 100,000, and Enterprise on quote above that. Micro covers automated scans and fixes every 24 hours, an accessibility statement and report, and email plus live chat. Growth adds a dedicated case manager, Google Analytics integration, multi-account and team management, and a two-day response target. Scale adds one hour with an ADA attorney, a yearly round of manual testing and validation by their experts, and custom fixes. Enterprise adds 5 to 10 attorney hours, on-demand testing, SSO and a dedicated account manager. The page opens on the yearly toggle, so $490 is the first figure a visitor sees; switching it to 'Billed monthly' shows the monthly rates above. Paying yearly saves about 30% against twelve monthly payments, which is what the toggle's own SAVE 30% label claims. No free plan and no trial is listed. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with accessiBe?

The thing you most need to know about this product is not on its pricing page, it is on the FTC’s. In January 2025 the Federal Trade Commission ordered accessiBe to pay $1 million to settle allegations that it misrepresented what accessWidget does; the Commission approved the order as final in April 2025. The FTC’s position was that accessiBe’s claim that its AI plug-in could make any website conform to WCAG was false, misleading or unsubstantiated — the widget did not make all customer sites WCAG-compliant. The order now bars the company from claiming its automated products make a site WCAG-compliant, or keep it compliant over time, unless it holds evidence to back that up. It also requires disclosing the domain limitations of those products before you incur a financial obligation. There is a second finding that belongs on a page like this one. The FTC also alleged accessiBe formatted third-party articles and reviews so they read as independent opinion while failing to disclose the company’s material connections to those reviewers; the order requires it to disclose such connections from here on. We are an affiliate of accessiBe and we get paid if you sign up through our link — which is exactly the sort of connection that case was about, and exactly why it is stated here rather than in a footnote. What that means in practice: price this as a partial aid, not as legal cover. An overlay sits on top of a site and adjusts it in the browser. It cannot rewrite bad markup, fix an inaccessible custom component or make a keyboard trap go away, and no vendor’s automated layer can promise the conformance a real audit and remediation deliver. If you are buying to reduce the chance of a demand letter, buy the audit — the widget is not that. And the structural point behind the FTC order: accessWidget adjusts the page in the browser at load time rather than fixing the underlying markup, so the remediation exists only while the widget is being served. Stop paying and the site is exactly as accessible as it was before you started — which is the difference between renting a compliance layer and fixing a site.

ADP

Is ADP worth it?

ADP is genuinely best for small and mid-sized employers who want payroll, tax filing and compliance handled by the largest and oldest provider in the category: over 75 years, and their own figure of more than 900,000 small businesses on RUN alone. It suits those who would rather have a named account team than a cheaper self-service tool. Skip it if you need to budget before you talk to sales, because you simply cannot: every route ends at a quote form. Also skip it if you are a one-or-two-person company where a published-price competitor covers the same job, and if you only pay 1099 contractors, price that specific product rather than the packages, because ADP sells it separately.

How much does ADP cost?

(2026-08-15, read on their own site) ADP shows no public pricing for RUN anywhere on its site, and we checked four places before saying so. /pricing returns a 404, and so does /pricing.md. The RUN product page offers only a “Get Pricing” button. And the dedicated package-comparison page lists all four tiers with “Get pricing” under each one and not a single figure. The tiers are Essential Payroll, Enhanced Payroll (adds ZipRecruiter, State Unemployment Insurance and garnishment payments), Complete Payroll & HR Plus (adds basic HR support) and HR Pro Payroll & HR (enhanced HR support and staff perks). A promotion was running when we read it: three months of free payroll, marked “Ends 8/27”, with terms behind an asterisk. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with ADP?

The quote-only posture is normal in payroll, so the honest complaint is not that ADP hides its price — it is what that does to you as a buyer. You cannot compare ADP against anyone without first giving a salesperson your headcount and your phone number. And the three-months-free banner carries an end date, which puts a clock on a decision you cannot yet cost. Ask for the per-employee-per-month rate and the per-payroll-run fee separately. Ask what happens in month four. And ask what the price becomes at double your current headcount, because payroll pricing steps rather than scales smoothly. One detail worth noticing: ADP does publish an /llms.txt, a file whose entire purpose is to guide AI assistants around its site. They are willing to machine-readably explain what they sell. They are not willing to say what it costs.

Airia

Is Airia worth it?

Airia is genuinely best for enterprises that need to find the AI already running across their organisation, enforce policy on it at runtime, and build and govern their own agents, with auditability and compliance evidence built in. Skip it if you're an individual or small team wanting a single AI assistant (this is far more platform than you need), or you can't commit to an enterprise rollout.

How much does Airia cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-10) Quote-only. Airia used to publish a ladder: a free tier, Individual around $50 a month and Team around $250. That page is gone — both airia.com/pricing and www.airia.com/pricing now redirect to airia.ai/docs/build/interface-options/usage_limits, a developer documentation page titled Usage Limits on a different domain, while airia.ai/pricing answers 200 with a shell that carries no text at all without JavaScript, and we found no amount anywhere on the site on 10 September 2026. Every deployment is therefore quoted after a sales conversation. Treat any $50 or $250 figure still in circulation, including in older versions of this review, as historical rather than current. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Airia?

Airia used to publish part of its ladder, which was refreshingly rare for enterprise AI. As of 20 August 2026 that is over: the pricing page redirects into developer documentation on airia.ai from both airia.com and www.airia.com, and there is no amount anywhere on the site. So the honest position is now the usual one for this category — you cannot budget before a sales conversation. The underlying fit has not changed, though the pitch has. Read on 1 September 2026, the site leads with discovery and enforcement rather than building: finding the AI tools, models, agents and MCP servers already running, including the unapproved ones, stopping actions at the execution layer before the tool call fires, and mapping governance evidence to the EU AI Act, NIST AI RMF, SR 11-7, HIPAA, ISO 42001 and SOC 2. The agent builder now sits under Optimize. Either way this only pays off at organisational scale. A small team that wants one chatbot is buying far more platform than it needs. For an enterprise standardising and governing AI, that is exactly the point.

Airwallex

Is Airwallex worth it?

Airwallex is genuinely best for companies invoicing or paying in several currencies, where a 0.5% spread against your bank's 2–3% is the whole business case. Skip it if you bill in one currency and pay domestic suppliers — the multi-currency machinery is the product, and without FX volume you are paying a platform fee for a bank account.

How much does Airwallex cost?

(2026-08-28, read off their own pricing page and fee table; no rate below has changed since 1 Aug 2026) FX conversion 0.5% above the interbank rate for major currencies and 1% for all others. Local transfers to 120+ countries free; SWIFT transfers $15–$25 each. Payment acceptance 2.8% + $0.30 on domestic cards and 4.30% + $0.30 on international cards; local payment methods $0.30 plus method-specific fees. Corporate cards carry no international fees and up to 2% cash rebate on local USD spend. Account plans: Explore free, Grow $12 per active Spend user/month plus a platform fee that varies with team size, Accelerate custom. Their table adds limits the earlier reading did not record: the first 10 Spend users are free and the $12 rate runs to 250 Spend users; the monthly fee is charged per business legal entity, with up to 3 entities included and $1,000 per additional entity; subscription and usage-based billing cost 0.50% per successful transaction while invoicing is free; and USD balances earn a Yield rate that Airwallex restamps daily rather than fixing per plan — on 5 September 2026 the page showed 3.22% on Explore, 3.37% on Grow and 3.49% on Accelerate, and its own footnote reads that the indicative rate is the 7-day annualised historical rate of the underlying funds as of 4 September 2026, net of fees. Read those three figures as a moving number carrying the date the page gave it, not as a plan feature: they were 3.21%, 3.36% and 3.48% when we first recorded them on 28 August 2026. (yield re-verified 5 Sep 2026) Platform API and embedded finance are quote-only. Two gaps we had left open got a partial answer on 17 September 2026, when Airwallex pointed us at its fee schedule. Dispute handling does carry a price, but only the Canadian entity publishes it: airwallex.com/en-ca/terms/fee-schedule prints 15 CAD per chargeback request and 0.30 CAD per refund request, both excluding whatever the card network passes on. The US pricing page lists dispute management as a feature with no fee beside it, so treat the Canadian figures as the shape of the charge rather than as your rate. The Grow platform fee is still not a number anywhere: both the US pricing page and the Canadian schedule say only that it is based on the total number of Spend users, so ask for it before you budget. Re-verified 2026-09-19 on airwallex.com/en-us/pricing: every published rate above is unchanged — FX, transfers, acquiring, the 0.50% on subscription billing, the free first 10 Spend users, the 250-user ceiling on the $12 rate and the $1,000 per entity beyond three. The yield figures still read 3.22%, 3.37% and 3.49%, but their footnote now dates the seven-day annualised rate to 18 September 2026 instead of 4 September. That is the clearest evidence yet that these three numbers are a daily restamp rather than a plan feature: same values, new date. The Grow platform fee is still not printed anywhere. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Airwallex?

The honest headline here is that the FX markup is published at all — 0.5% and 1% above interbank is a real number, and most competitors will not print one. What the page does not settle is everything around it. The Grow plan's platform fee 'varies with team size' with no table, so the $12 a user is not the whole subscription -- though their table does say the first ten Spend users are free, which is the part the headline rate hides in your favour. Chargeback and dispute fees are not priced anywhere, only mentioned as a feature. And the page's own disclaimer says per-transaction fees apply to certain transactions with the detail in a separate fee schedule. So: use the published FX rate to compare against your bank, then ask for the fee schedule and the platform fee before you move money.

Read the full Airwallex review →

Alohi

Is Alohi worth it?

Alohi is genuinely best for businesses that need secure online faxing, e-signatures or scanning — especially regulated ones needing HIPAA-compliant fax on the Enterprise tier. Skip it if you only need occasional e-signatures (a single dedicated tool may be cheaper) or your document volume is low enough for free options.

How much does Alohi cost?

(read 2026-09-10 on fax.plus/pricing and sign.plus/pricing) Priced per product, in USD, with a currency switcher offering EUR, CHF and GBP. IMPORTANT: on Fax.Plus the '/month' figure on each card is the annual rate. Fax.Plus — Basic $6.99 a month billed as $83.99 annually (200 pages a month, one fax number, one member, $0.10 per extra page, 60 pages maximum per fax); Premium $13.99 / $167.99 (500 pages, $0.07 per extra page, 90 pages per fax, adds toll-free numbers, number porting, CSV log export and the ability to switch off reception); Business $27.99 / $335.99 (1,000 pages); Enterprise $79.99 a month on annual billing, $959.99 a year, and the only card that also shows a monthly-billing rate, 'starts at $99.99' (4,000 pages, multiple fax numbers, unlimited members, $0.03 per extra page, 250 pages per fax, 1,000 AI credits a month, SSO, HIPAA-compliant with BAA, the Fax.Plus API, data residency and Zapier). The banner offers 'up to 3 months free with an annual plan (save up to 22%)'. A free plan gives 10 pages in total, not per month. Extra fax numbers are $4.99 a month (Business and Enterprise include two); vanity numbers $19.99; choosing your own number within a region is a one-time $19.99, or $29.99 from their handpicked memorable set. Porting is free but country-dependent — porting a US or Canadian number requires Premium or higher. The footnotes warn that faxes to certain destinations may count as multiple pages, priced on a separate Sending Rates page, and that taxes are excluded. Sign.Plus is a separate subscription and prints annual and monthly rates side by side: Free (3 signature requests in total, 1 template, 1 user), Personal $9.99 annual / $14.99 monthly, $119.99 a year (1 user, 10 signature requests a month, 5 templates), Professional $19.99 / $29.99 per user, $239.99 a year (up to 5 users, unlimited requests, 10 templates), Business $29.99 / $49.99 per user, $359.99 a year (unlimited templates, document sharing, Zapier, custom branding), Enterprise $49.99 / $79.99 per user, $599.99 a year (SSO, HIPAA-compliant with BAA, advanced security controls, data residency, priority support). Sign.Plus advertises 'save up to 40% with annual plans'. Request via SMS/WhatsApp is metered separately at 10, 30 and 150 a month from Professional upward. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Alohi?

The honest catch is that Alohi is a suite of separate products — Fax.Plus, Sign.Plus, Scan.Plus — each with its own subscription, so "one ecosystem" can mean several line items if you want e-signatures and faxing and scanning. Page and member limits on the lower tiers are modest, and HIPAA/BAA sits only on the top tier. For a single job (say online faxing), it's clean and cheap; for the full workflow, add up the parts.

Read the full Alohi review →

Alvys

Is Alvys worth it?

Alvys is genuinely best for FTL trucking carriers and asset-based (hybrid) brokers with roughly 5–300 trucks — especially compliance-heavy freight like hazmat, reefer and cross-border — plus shippers running private fleets, with real back-office staff to put dispatch, billing and DOT/IFTA compliance in one system. Skip it if you're a pure-play broker without assets, you run LTL/multi-modal loads billing multiple customers per load, you need rail, air or ocean tracking, or you're a single-truck operator without back-office staff.

How much does Alvys cost?

(re-verified 20 Sep 2026) The pricing page is public again, and it has tiers now. On 26 August 2026 alvys.com/pricing answered an ordinary request with HTTP 401 and sat behind a password; read on 20 September 2026 it returns 200 and shows three plans — Foundations, Growth and Scale — none of which carries an amount. All three read "Quoted based on volume of loads", under the page's own summary line: simple per-load pricing, free implementation, cancel anytime. Foundations now has a 14-day free trial behind a Try for free button; Growth and Scale route to sales. The FAQ states there are no setup fees, no per-user or per-seat charge, no contracts and month-to-month cancellation. Two extras are priced in the open: EDI includes 2 trading partners on Growth and 5 on Scale with per-partner pricing above the cap, and the developer sandbox is a $100/month add-on on Foundations and Growth, included on Scale. Custom reports, scheduled reports, Insights AI analytics, Sage and Samsara Premium are add-ons on every tier. The last published load-based rates (2026) were Carrier from ~$150/mo and Broker from ~$300/mo; those tier names no longer exist on the page and the figures are history, not a quote. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Alvys?

Two honest boundaries. Implementation runs to weeks — the vendor's own pricing FAQ says most operations are live in under two to four weeks — and real-time support is the top tier's: self-serve on Foundations, chat and email on Growth, phone and a dedicated success manager only on Scale. And it's built specifically for truck-based FTL operations: if you're a pure-play (non-asset) broker, run LTL with multiple customers per load, or need rail/air/ocean tracking, this isn't your TMS — Alvys itself is refreshingly explicit about that. The fit is narrow and deep, not broad.

Aplos

Is Aplos worth it?

Aplos is genuinely best for small nonprofits and faith-based organisations that need true fund accounting rather than general small-business bookkeeping — tracking restricted and unrestricted funds separately is the thing generic tools genuinely cannot do. Skip it if you are a normal for-profit business, because you are paying for fund accounting you will never use; also skip it if two users on the entry tier does not cover your team and the next tier up is out of budget.

How much does Aplos cost?

(2026-09-10, read on their own pricing page) Three published tiers, billed monthly: Lite at $79/month, Core at $129 and Advanced at $229, plus a fourth Custom tier with tailored pricing aimed at organisations above $1 million in annual revenue. Advanced is framed as a quote rather than a checkout: the card reads "Customized solutions start at $229/month" and the button asks for a demo. A promotion was running when we re-read the page on 10 September 2026 — 50% off for three months with code FALL26, showing Lite at $39.50 and Core at $64.50 — and the footnote dates it: new customers only, ending 30 September. The earlier SUMMER26 promotion that ran to 31 August has been replaced on the same terms. What matters is still what you pay in month four. The trial is 15 days and takes no credit card. Lite and Core each include 2 users; Advanced includes 3. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Aplos?

The headline you will see first is not the price you will pay. The 50%-off banner sits at the top of the page and rewrites every figure on it. So the $39.50 that catches your eye is a three-month rate against a $79 list price. That is a 100% increase waiting in month four, on software that holds your books and is therefore painful to leave. The second thing to pin down is users: Lite includes two, and for a nonprofit with a bookkeeper, a treasurer and a director that is already tight, so establish the per-user cost before you commit.

Read the full Aplos review →

Arbor

Is Arbor worth it?

Arbor is genuinely best for US households and renters in deregulated states who want set-and-forget savings on the electric bill without becoming rate-shopping hobbyists. Skip it if you live outside the 13 deregulated states, are on a municipal or co-op utility, or genuinely enjoy comparing supplier offers yourself each year.

How much does Arbor cost?

Free for households, and re-verified as such on 2026-08-17. Arbor has no pricing page at all — the URL 404s — and neither our July nor our August 2026 capture produced a figure. That is consistent with the model: Arbor is paid a referral fee by the winning electricity supplier, not by you. US-only: it works in the 13 deregulated 'energy choice' states across the Northeast, Midwest and Texas, and only on the supply portion of your bill. The delivery charges from your utility are untouched, so the saving applies to part of the bill rather than to all of it. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Arbor?

The honest catches: it only exists in deregulated US states, and it only touches the supply rate, since delivery charges stay with your utility. The headline savings — Arbor itself reports an average of up to $593/year — depend entirely on how bad your current default rate is. If you already shopped your rate recently, the gain may be small.

Read the full Arbor review →

Aspire

Is Aspire worth it?

Aspire is genuinely best for startups and SMBs that want an all-in-one business account with $0 monthly fees and built-in spend management. Skip it if you need a traditional bank relationship, lending, or coverage in a region Aspire doesn't serve — check availability first.

How much does Aspire cost?

(price re-verified 2026-09-11 against the vendor's own pricing page) SGD $0 monthly fee on Basic, no minimum balance and no deposit to open. You pay per use: FX from 0.46% to send and from 0.34% to receive, inbound SWIFT SGD $35 for SGD accounts or USD $8 for others, outbound SWIFT USD $15 for SHA and USD $30 for OURS. Premium is SGD $15/month and zeroes the FX margin on the first SGD $13,000 of conversions a month, after which the same from-0.46% applies. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Aspire?

It's a fintech account (strongest in Southeast Asia), not a full bank — great for keeping fixed costs near zero on mostly-domestic activity, but confirm it supports your country and currencies before you build your finances around it.

Read the full Aspire review →

Assembly

Is Assembly worth it?

Assembly is genuinely best for agencies and professional services firms who want one branded place for clients to message, sign, pay and share files instead of four tools and an email thread. Skip it if you have a handful of clients and already use a shared drive and an invoicing tool, because the value here is the single branded portal and that only matters once clients get confused about where things live.

How much does Assembly cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-17 in the browser) Assembly publishes exactly one price: Empower at $3 per user per month, billed yearly, with an Enterprise tier quoted on request for advanced security, custom integrations and tailored implementation. There is no monthly/yearly toggle and no other published tier. Empower covers milestone celebrations, announcements, awards and challenges, the global reward catalogue, peer-to-peer recognition, points and budget controls, the Dora Hub AI insights and advanced people analytics. Note that the reward points themselves are funded by you on top of the seat price, so budget the catalogue separately from the licence. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Assembly?

Two meters run at once and they move at different speeds. Clients is the one that bites first: Starter stops at 50, and the step to 500 costs $70 more a month. Internal users is the other, and it gets worse as you climb. An extra person is $29 on Professional and $59 on Advanced, so the more expensive plan makes each additional colleague more expensive too — the opposite of what a tier ladder usually does. The last step is the steepest in the category: $499 to a $2,000 minimum, and what that buys is SSO and security controls rather than more capacity.

Read the full Assembly review →

Atto

Is Atto worth it?

Atto is genuinely best for hourly and mobile crews (construction, cleaning, field service) that want dead-simple clock-in/out, GPS-verified timesheets and payroll-ready exports. Skip it if office/knowledge teams (project-based trackers like Toggl fit better), or anywhere the team hasn't been brought along on why.

How much does Atto cost?

(re-read 2026-09-05 on attotime.com/pricing) Per user per month, billed annually: Starter $5.65, Plus $7.95 (job codes, breaks and notes), Advanced $10.75 (job costing), Expert $16.55 (talk to sales). Payroll processing is an add-on at $5.95 per payroll user a month plus a $39.95 monthly base fee. No base price and no setup fee on the plans themselves, unlimited jobs and locations, and a free sign-up to start. History: our earlier read put Starter at about $6.95 and the top tier at $19.95; the current ladder is lower on every rung. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Atto?

Simple and honest pricing — the real conversation with any field-team tracker is the human one: GPS tracking of employees needs transparency and often consent (rules differ by country), and adoption dies the day the crew feels surveilled rather than served. Roll it out as 'accurate paychecks and fewer timesheet fights', involve the team, and the tool pays for itself; roll it out as surveillance and no feature list saves it.

BackupMaster

Is BackupMaster worth it?

BackupMaster is genuinely best for Shopify stores under roughly 1,500 orders a month that want the whole store backed up daily — metafields, metaobjects and translations included — without anyone having to remember to trigger it. Skip it if you need backups exported to storage you control on the entry tier, an on-demand backup before every risky change, or a written list of what restore does not cover.

How much does BackupMaster cost?

(read 2026-08-22 on apps.shopify.com/backup-master) Four tiers, each paid one with a 7-day free trial. Agency is free to install but only for Shopify Partner development stores before they are transferred to a client, with all features enabled for three months. Essentials $19 a month or $205.20 a year, a 10% saving, covers stores up to 300 orders a month. That is automatic daily backups of products, collections, themes, blogs, pages, files, metafields, metaobjects, translations, customers, orders and inventories, restore of supported items, 365-day retention and unlimited cloud storage. Pro $39 a month or $421.20 a year covers up to 600 orders a month and adds downloadable backup copies, Google Drive sync and duplication of data for staging or expansion. Plus $79 a month or $853.20 a year covers up to 1,500 orders a month, and adds multi-store pricing, detailed audit logs, external portal access, a custom backup time and priority support. The listing says higher-volume plans exist on the vendor's own pricing page without printing what they cost. All charges are billed in USD and recur every 30 days. Developer BackupMaster, Bondi, NSW, Australia; the app launched 4 August 2017, carries Shopify's “Built for Shopify” badge, is available in English and German, and had 108 reviews at the time of writing. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with BackupMaster?

The listing header says free to install, and there is a free tier, but it is not for you unless you are an agency: Agency is limited to Shopify Partner development stores before they are handed over to a client. For a live store the entry price is $19 a month and it buys 300 orders a month. Restore is described throughout as restoring supported items, and nowhere does the listing say which items are not supported. That phrase is carrying a lot of weight in a product whose entire promise is that you can undo a mistake. Ask for the exclusion list before you rely on it, and run one real restore into a development store rather than discovering the boundary during an incident. The most useful review on the page is the critical one. In April 2026 a merchant asked for exports over SFTP to storage the vendor does not control, on-demand backups before risky changes, configurable retention and languages beyond English and German. The same merchant noted that a second store costs extra here where some rivals include one. None of that is damning; it is a fair map of where this app currently stops. Worth adding to the same note: your store data sits with a vendor in Australia, which belongs in your processing register whatever you decide.

Barcodes Talk

Is Barcodes Talk worth it?

Barcodes Talk is genuinely best for sellers and small brands listing on marketplaces or in independent retail who want authentic, one-time-cost UPC/EAN barcodes without an annual GS1 membership. Skip it if your target retailer or brand registry explicitly requires a GS1-issued company prefix in your own name — then buy direct from GS1.

How much does Barcodes Talk cost?

(prices re-verified 2026-09-19 on barcodestalk.com/buy-barcodes) One-off purchase, no subscription and no renewal. The full published ladder runs eighteen rungs: 1 barcode $5.00; 5 for $10.00 ($2.00 each); 10 for $15.00 ($1.50); 25 for $25.00 ($1.00); 50 for $35.00 ($0.70); 100 for $45.00 ($0.45); 200 for $80.00 ($0.40); 500 for $125.00 ($0.25); 1,000 for $180.00 ($0.18); 1,500 for $225.00 ($0.15); 2,000 for $260.00 ($0.13); 2,500 for $300.00 ($0.12); 5,000 for $500.00 ($0.10); 10,000 for $650.00 ($0.065); 25,000 for $1,200.00 ($0.048); 50,000 for $2,300.00 ($0.046); 75,000 for $3,225.00 ($0.043); and 100,000 for $3,500.00 ($0.035). Larger quantities are quote-only. The unit price drops fastest between one and a hundred codes. Every purchase includes both UPC and EAN formats, high-resolution EPS and JPEG images, a Certificate of GTIN Assignment, Excel and PDF spreadsheets of the assigned numbers, instant download, and the vendor's Works for Amazon Guarantee. Unchanged from our 5 September reading. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Barcodes Talk?

The honest caveat is about fit, not the codes. Some large retailers and brand registries — notably certain Amazon Brand Registry and big-box requirements — increasingly ask for a GS1-issued prefix tied to your own company, and a third-party barcode (legitimate and globally unique as it is) may not satisfy that specific gatekeeping. For most marketplaces and independent retail it's completely fine and far cheaper; just confirm your channel's exact requirement first.

Bill.com

Is Bill.com worth it?

Bill.com is genuinely best for growing companies with real accounts-payable volume and approval chains that want to automate bill pay and sync to accounting. Skip it if you pay only a few bills a month — your accounting software or a free tool like Melio's base plan covers it without per-seat fees.

How much does Bill.com cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-15 on their own pricing page) Accounts-payable and receivable plans priced per user: Essentials $49, Team $65 and Corporate $89 per user per month, plus a quote-only Enterprise - the same four cards as on 15 August 2026. The separate Spend and Expense product is $0 per user per month and carries credit lines the page advertises as $1,000-$5M, not guaranteed and set on approval. Transaction fees stack on top of the seat, and are where the real cost hides. ACH or ePayment is $0.59, a mailed check $1.99, an international USD wire $19.99, and an international FX wire or a virtual card is free - free of a stated fee, that is, because the same row says the exchange rate applies and BILL only discloses that rate at the time of the transaction. Instant payment is 1.0% with a $9.99 minimum and a $100 maximum, and Pay Faster ACH $11.99. Expedited checks are $24.99 overnight, $19.99 two-day and $14.99 three-day. Paying by card costs 2.9%, and the receiver pays 2.9% on credit or debit card payments; a receiver taking an instant transfer pays 1% to 1.49% with a $1 minimum. A second fee block, easy to miss under the payment table, prices the paperwork and the mistakes: 1099 e-filing to the IRS is $2.99 per form ($1.99 through an accountant console), state filing $1.49 and mailing a 1099 to the vendor $1.99, while voiding a check is $25, a returned check $3, a re-debit after failed funding $25 and an ACH that fails after the receiver has been paid $50. The accountant side is priced separately again: a BILL AP & AR Partner subscription at $49 a month, an Accountant Console the FAQ also prices at $49 a month, and a 10-20% client subscription discount that scales with how many clients you onboard. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Bill.com?

Per-user pricing plus per-transaction fees means the real cost depends on team size and payment volume — it's built for finance teams with genuine AP workflows and is overkill if you pay a handful of bills a month.

Is BillingNow worth it?

BillingNow is genuinely best for freelancers and small teams that want unlimited invoicing, expenses and client management with zero per-user maths — users are unlimited on both plans, so a five-person team pays $2.00 a head. Skip it if you need accountant-grade features, payroll or deep integrations (the incumbents' ecosystems are bigger), or you have an existing client list to bring across — the help centre documents export but no import, so every client has to be retyped.

How much does BillingNow cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-20, read on their own site) Two plans, both carrying the identical feature list with no limits: $9.99 a month, or $99.90 a year which the page itself works out to $8.33 a month and labels 'Save 17%'. Both include unlimited invoices, expenses, client relations, users, estimates and credit notes, plus payment tracking, tax rates, PDF export and the AI assistant. There are no per-user fees and no feature-gated tier. The trial is 3 days and the page states three separate times that no credit card is required to start it. SOC 2 Type II certified with European-standard compliant invoices. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with BillingNow?

The pricing is genuinely refreshing — two plans, same features on both, unlimited users, no per-seat maths. The honest watch-out is the trial length rather than its terms: three days is short for migrating billing into a new tool, and the practical constraint is that there is no import path. The help centre documents CSV and Excel export but no import, so an existing client list has to be retyped — which is more than three days of work for anyone with a real book. It is also a young product from a small Geneva maker, so weigh ecosystem depth and longevity before you move your billing history in.

Bitvavo

Is Bitvavo worth it?

Bitvavo is genuinely best for EU-based traders and builders who want a MiCA-regulated, euro-native exchange with low tiered fees, deep EUR pairs and a solid API for automation. Skip it if you're outside the EU (it's euro-centric), you trade exotic altcoins it doesn't list, or you need derivatives-first infrastructure.

How much does Bitvavo cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-18, read on their own fees page) No subscription — you pay per trade. EUR markets (Category A) run ten volume tiers. It is 0.15% maker / 0.25% taker below €100,000 of 30-day volume, then 0.10%/0.20% from €100,000, 0.09%/0.18% from €250,000, 0.08%/0.16% from €500,000 and 0.05%/0.14% from €1,000,000. Above that: 0.03%/0.12% from €2,500,000, 0.01%/0.10% from €5,000,000, 0.00%/0.08% from €10,000,000, 0.00%/0.04% from €25,000,000 and 0.00%/0.02% above €100,000,000. USDC markets (Category B) are a separate schedule from 0.05% maker and taker, and stablecoin pairs (Category C) from 0.10%; Bitvavo states that stablecoin trades do not count towards the 30-day volume that sets your tier. Deposits are free by SEPA, iDEAL/Wero, Apple Pay and Bancontact; a credit card costs 1%, PayPal 2% and EPS or Giropay 1.75%. Margin positions carry a separate hourly borrowing fee, published at 0.0023% an hour on every listed asset, plus a 2% fee if the position is liquidated. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Bitvavo?

The honest caveats are crypto-wide, not Bitvavo-specific: fees are only part of the cost (spread matters on small orders), and no exchange removes market risk — the platform being cheap and MiCA-regulated doesn't make the assets safe. Note the disclosure: this is one of the few tools here we genuinely run — our own trading system executes on Bitvavo — which is experience, but also means we're an actual customer, not a neutral bystander.

Bolt Business

Is Bolt Business worth it?

Bolt Business is genuinely best for companies with European/African travel footprints that want employee rides centrally billed, policy-controlled and expense-integrated — at zero platform cost. Skip it if you operate in the US only (network coverage), or you need guaranteed fixed-price corporate transport contracts.

How much does Bolt Business cost?

No subscription, no activation cost and no minimum commitment (re-verified 2026-08-20 on Bolt's own business page): you pay per ride or rental taken, with consolidated monthly billing, spend limits and policy controls included. One line the marketing does not carry, but Bolt's own support pages do: Bolt charges a service fee on every trip made with the business payment method, billed at the start of the following month. On credit card it is deducted automatically and does not appear on the individual trip invoice — it arrives as a separate service fee statement on the 3rd of each month; on postpaid it sits on the monthly invoice. What is the catch with Bolt Business?

The honest boundary is geography: Bolt's network is Europe/Africa-centric across 600+ cities, so US-only operations should look elsewhere, and ride availability varies by city — pilot it on your team's actual routes. Pricing is dynamic like all ride-hailing; the business layer adds control and receipts, not fixed corporate fares, so budget-predictability comes from your spend limits, not from locked prices.

(price re-verified 2026-09-14 on browse.ai/pricing, both billing columns read) Credit-metered (2026): Free ~50 credits/mo (2 sites); Personal about $19/mo annual (12,000 credits/yr, 5 sites); Professional ~$69/mo annual (60,000 credits, 10 sites); Premium custom (600k+ credits, managed). A credit ≈ 10 extracted rows; premium sites cost more credits. Seats come with the plan: 3 users on Free and Personal, 10 on Professional. Extra websites cost $4/mo on Personal and $2.40/mo on Professional, paid annually. Bigger credit buckets are sold inside a plan rather than by upgrading: Personal 24,000 credits a year at $38/mo, Professional 120,000 at $129, 240,000 at $239 and 360,000 at $319. Credits reset at the end of the billing cycle and do not carry over unless you upgrade first; data retention is 90 days on all three self-serve plans, and the shortest monitor interval is hourly on Free against 5 minutes on the paid plans. Watch out for one vendor-side inconsistency: browse.ai/llms.txt, the file AI assistants are pointed at, still lists Starter $49, Professional $149 and Team $399 plans that do not appear anywhere on the pricing page (both read 14 September 2026). Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Browse.ai?

Two honest realities of no-code scraping. Credits burn faster than expected: row counts, screenshots and 'premium' sites all multiply the meter, so monitor a big site daily and do the math first. And scrapers break when sites redesign — robots need maintenance, which is your time even when the tool is no-code. Also check the terms of the sites you monitor; 'technically possible' and 'permitted' aren't the same thing.

Is BuddyPunch worth it?

BugHerd

Is BugHerd worth it?

BugHerd is genuinely best for web agencies and site teams that want clients to point-and-click feedback directly on the page — and, new in 2026, want AI agents (Claude Code, Cursor and others) to read, triage and action that feedback via BugHerd's MCP server, free in beta. Skip it if you don't work with external clients or website revisions (a plain issue tracker is enough), or you need deep dev-workflow tracking that belongs in Jira.

How much does BugHerd cost?

Standard about $42/mo (5 members), Studio ~$67/mo (10), Premium ~$125/mo (25), plus custom Enterprise — all with unlimited projects and unlimited client users; annual billing runs $500/$800/$1,500 per year (two months free), extra members $6.60/user/mo billed annually ($80 a year each). Free trial. Prices in US dollars before tax (re-verified 2026-09-05; unchanged since our July read). The MCP server and the other AI features now sit inside the Standard plan on the pricing page, still marked beta. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with BugHerd?

The per-team-member tiers are the thing to size honestly: your clients report for free as guests, but everyone on your side who works the feedback needs a seat, so a growing agency steps up tiers. It's deliberately a visual-feedback layer, not a full project-management or dev-issue tracker — most teams run it alongside Jira/Trello rather than instead of. The new MCP feature is genuinely interesting but currently beta: treat AI-agents-triaging-your-feedback as a bonus to evaluate, not the reason to buy.

Bybit

Is Bybit worth it?

Bybit is genuinely best for active traders who want deep liquidity, low derivatives fees and a broad product range (spot, perps, options) with volume-tiered discounts. Skip it if you're a buy-and-hold beginner (a simple euro-native exchange is safer territory), or derivatives aren't available to you locally.

How much does Bybit cost?

No subscription — per-trade fees, re-verified 2026-09-05 against Bybit's own fee schedule, which the page says was last updated on 2 September 2026. At VIP 0 (non-VIP): spot 0.1000% maker and 0.1000% taker; perpetuals and futures 0.0200% maker and 0.0550% taker; options 0.0200% maker and 0.0300% taker. The VIP ladder cuts those toward zero — at Supreme VIP spot is 0.0300% maker and 0.0450% taker, derivatives 0.0000% maker and 0.0300% taker, options 0.0050% and 0.0150%. Note that the top derivatives maker rate is zero rather than a rebate: we found no negative maker fee in the published table. That base table is not the only one, and the others start higher: fiat-crypto spot pairs cost 0.2000% taker and 0.1500% maker under $100,000 of 30-day volume, the Spot Adventure Zone and xStocks 0.2000% both ways at VIP 0, and Pre-Market Perpetuals and the Innovation Zone 0.0400% maker and 0.1000% taker — so a new account trading the newest listings pays about double the headline rate. Your VIP level is set by your asset balance, your 30-day average net borrowing or your trading volume over the past 30 days, whichever qualifies you higher, and it refreshes daily at 07:00 UTC. Bybit adds that actual rates can vary by region and points you to your own My Fee Rate page. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Bybit?

Derivatives are the draw and the danger: leverage cuts both ways, and funding rates on perpetuals are a real recurring cost the fee table doesn't show. Regulation-wise, check what Bybit may offer in YOUR jurisdiction before funding an account — availability and product range differ per country, and that's the part most fee comparisons skip.

Carbon6

Is Carbon6 worth it?

Carbon6 is genuinely best for Amazon sellers who want reimbursements, advertising, inventory and sourcing tools from one vendor and are content to buy them tool by tool — the suite trades under the Carbon6 brand again since 30 Jun 2026, so there is a named counterparty on the contract. Skip it if you want one flat subscription and one invoice: this is a suite priced per tool, each with its own contract, and the pieces have moved between owners twice since Feb 2025 — check which entity your existing agreement actually names before you add another one.

How much does Carbon6 cost?

What we can verify on the vendor’s own pages today (re-checked 26 Aug 2026): carbon6.io/pricing now redirects to the tool list, so there is no suite-level price at all. SoStocked has dropped its two-tier ladder — where on 21 Jul it listed ProfitFlow at $97/mo and ProfitFlow + Inventory at "starting at" $250/mo, it now shows a single Inventory + Profit plan at "starting at" $175/mo with a free trial. ScanUnlimited lists a free Limited tier and Unlimited at $70/mo, or $60/mo on the annual plan ($720/yr, marketed as two months free); the Unlimited Plus tier that sat above it at $1,080/yr is no longer on the pricing page. The off-Amazon advertising tool is listed as PixelMe by Carbon6 again rather than SPS Ad Performance, and it publishes no price. The 3P FBA-reimbursement service is a contingency arrangement: Seller Investigators by Carbon6 states a 25% commission charged only after Amazon has deposited the recovered funds, stated twice on its own site (re-read 26 Aug 2026), after a free 48-hour audit with no obligation. Our older suite-level figures — entry tools from about $49/mo, PixelMe from about $500/mo with a 6-month minimum, and $300-500+/mo for a working stack — stay withdrawn: none of those is verifiable against a live authoritative source today. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Carbon6?

The honest headline here is not pricing but ownership — and it has moved again, this time in the reader's favour. SPS Commerce bought Carbon6 for roughly $210M on 7 Feb 2025 and retired the brand into spscommerce.com. On 30 Jun 2026 it sold the entire 3P (Amazon marketplace seller) revenue-recovery business for $9.5M cash, booking an estimated $20M loss, and named no buyer at the time. There is a name now. The partner-programme notice we received on 17 Aug 2026, from Carbon6's own partnerships contact, states that SPS transferred ownership of the 3P business to Carbon6 and that commission terms and payouts are unchanged. It also states that existing links were redirected to the Carbon6 tool sites on 14 Aug 2026, and that the programme itself rebrands from SPS to Carbon6 on 24 Aug 2026. Reading carbon6.io on 17 Aug 2026 matches that: the whole suite is on sale there again as "by Carbon6". Read the two purchase numbers carefully, because they are not the same thing. The $210M bought the whole Carbon6 stack; the $9.5M was the 3P arm alone, and offloading it at a book loss seventeen months in still tells you how it performed. What remains undisclosed is who stands behind the Carbon6 entity itself, so the question is answered one level down and no further. Two more things to hold. Reuters reported on 23 Jun 2026 that SPS hired Morgan Stanley to explore a sale of itself under activist pressure, with talks early and no deal assured. And the 1P side is no longer a clean split, because SPS kept the deduction-recovery business it bought while Carbon6's own site now lists ChargeGuard for Amazon 1P vendor recovery. Ask which entity your 1P work would sit with before you sign. For 3P reimbursements the July advice has expired — you can name the counterparty on your contract again.

CloudTask

Is CloudTask worth it?

CloudTask is genuinely best for companies hiring GTM roles — SDRs, BDRs and similar — who want LATAM talent without setting up their own payroll and compliance in another country, and who value the 24-month replacement guarantee. Skip it if you only need one hire ever, where Direct Hire's one-time 20-30% fee is cleaner than a managed monthly rate; also skip it if you cannot get the all-in monthly rate in writing before you commit.

How much does CloudTask cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-19, read on their own pricing page) Two hiring models, both published, plus one deposit. A one-time $299 deposit starts your search — asked “Do you charge a monthly platform fee?” their FAQ answers “No. There is a one-time $299 deposit to start your search. That is the only fee outside the models above.” — and the deposit applies toward your buyout or your first placement. Direct Hire costs 20% to 30% of first-year base salary, paid once and quoted per role, with a 6-month replacement guarantee (one replacement, same job description) and US W2 employment available. Managed Staffing is billed as one all-in monthly rate per person covering salary, compliance, payroll and support — that rate is not published, and it is the number that decides what this costs. It carries a 24-month replacement guarantee with replacements not capped, and time and activity tracking on by default, which the hire agrees to before matching and you can switch off when you post the role. Graduating a managed hire onto your own payroll is possible after six months for a one-time placement fee quoted at that moment; no amount is published. Health and equipment benefits are available on request on Managed Staffing only. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with CloudTask?

The $299 is the honest part and also the small part: their FAQ calls it a one-time deposit to start a search, denies any monthly platform fee, and says it goes toward your first placement. What you will actually spend is the all-in monthly rate per hire, and that figure appears nowhere on the pricing page — you get it after you talk to them, once you are already invested in the process. Work out before you start what a comparable LATAM salary is on your own, so you can see what the managed layer costs on top. The exit is worth reading twice too. After six months you can graduate a managed hire onto your own payroll, but the placement fee for doing so is quoted at the time and no amount is published. So the price of keeping the person is a number you cannot check in advance.

Cloudways

Is Cloudways worth it?

Cloudways is genuinely best for agencies and PHP shops running many sites who want servers managed but not metered. One server holds as many sites as it can carry with no visit caps, billing is hourly and post-paid with no annual term, and Magento and Laravel are first-class alongside WordPress. It suits teams who genuinely do not want anyone patching kernels at 2am. Skip it if you are comfortable in a terminal, or you run a single small site. Going direct to the cloud provider with your own control panel keeps the server in your own account for materially less. And one low-traffic site is better served by shared hosting, or by a per-site managed host that bundles CDN and application support. Cloudways also has no email hosting of its own, reselling a Rackspace add-on instead.

How much does Cloudways cost?

(read 2026-08-26 on cloudways.com/en/pricing.php, DigitalOcean tab) No free tier, but a 3-day free trial with no credit card required. Billing is hourly and post-paid, invoiced in the first week of the following month, with no annual contract — and the hourly figure is the one that decides the bill: every card divides its monthly price by 672 hours, so $0.0208 an hour is the $14 plan. The page now opens on DigitalOcean's Premium (NVMe) column, where Micro is 1GB RAM / 1 vCPU / 25GB NVMe / 1TB bandwidth at $14/month, Small 2GB / 1 vCPU / 50GB NVMe / 2TB at $28, Medium 4GB / 2 vCPU / 80GB / 4TB at $54 and Large 8GB / 4 vCPU / 160GB / 5TB at $99. Two of those have moved since July: the entry machine is now half the size for three dollars more (July's Micro was 2GB / 50GB at $11), and the 8GB plan went from $88 to $99. Switch the column to Standard and that same 2GB / 50GB box reads $11 a month — but the hourly figure printed underneath it, $0.0357, works out to $24 at the 672-hour month the other cards use, so the two numbers on that one card cannot both be right; read the live estimate before you commit. Bought direct from DigitalOcean on the same day, an 8GB / 4 vCPU / 160GB / 5TB droplet lists at $0.07143 an hour, $48 a month. Add-ons are monthly and separate: Cloudflare Enterprise CDN from $4.99 per domain, Site Manager from $3 per app, Malware Protection from $4 per app, Rackspace email $1 per mailbox, DNS Made Easy $0.50 per domain, offsite backup storage $0.033 per GB. Advance Support is $100/month or 10% of your invoice, whichever is higher, with a limited-time flat rate of $25/month advertised on the page today; the $500 Premium Support tier we recorded in July is no longer listed there. Cloudways Autonomous, the separate autoscaling product, now prices at $35, $240 and $995 a month where July's page showed $99, $199 and $399, and it meters unique visits and bandwidth rather than RAM (30k visits and 100GB on Small, 70k and 200GB on Medium) with a 3-day free trial. Markups on AWS and Google Cloud still cannot be calculated because Cloudways does not publish the underlying instance types. A site-wide banner advertises 40% off for four months until 15 September 2026 — promotions move, so read the live price. US list prices, excluding tax. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Cloudways?

"Fully managed" here means the server, not your site — and that distinction is where the money is. Cloudways' own support page puts infrastructure and platform work in the included tier but treats application help as guidance only. Plugin and theme troubleshooting, database work and performance tuning sit behind Advanced Support at $100/month or 10% of your invoice, whichever is higher, on a six-month minimum. On a small server that add-on costs more than the server it supports. Content changes, custom code debugging and code audits stay out of scope on every tier, paid ones included. The deeper catch is ownership: Cloudways documents that you get no root access and cannot use your own cloud credentials, so the machine sits in Cloudways' account — you cannot fire the management layer and keep the server, you migrate. And read the billing terms before you rely on it: their help centre states that an unpaid invoice restricts server access and can lead to the server and its backups being deleted, with backups kept for only 14 days after deletion.

Is Compliancely worth it?

Compliancely is genuinely best for finance, tax and HR teams with a steady monthly volume of US identity and business checks, who would rather buy TIN matching, KYB, I-9 and sanctions screening behind one API than integrate three separate vendors. It suits those willing to run a sales conversation to price the modules beyond TIN matching. Skip it if you need a handful of one-off verifications a year. The cheapest plan still bills a monthly allowance whether you use it or not, and the per-match rate is at its worst down there. Also skip it if you need the full cost of a compliance stack on paper before you talk to a salesperson, since only the TIN-match tiers are published.

How much does Compliancely cost?

(2026-07-29, re-verified 2026-08-05, read in a live browser session on their own pricing page, both billing toggles) The published price list covers one product only: TIN-match volume. Billed annually, Essential 25 is $16.95/mo for 25 monthly TIN verifications ($0.67 per match), Essential 50 $32.54/mo ($0.65), Essential 75 $45.79/mo ($0.61), Plus 100 $57.99/mo ($0.57) and Plus 250 $135.04/mo ($0.54). Switch the toggle to monthly billing and the same five tiers read $19.95, $38.49, $53.95, $67.95 and $159.95 per month, at $0.79 down to $0.63 per match. Everything else Compliancely sells carries no public price at all: the business verification and credit-risk report packages, address verification, Form I-9, IRS tax transcripts and income verification each end in Contact Sales or Book a Demo rather than a number. They confirmed on 11 August 2026 that buyers over that line are sent to their demo-request form at compliancely.com/demo-request, where the quote is written by hand; there is no published rate card for that range at all. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Compliancely?

The pricing page prices one product and the marketing sells about a dozen. Every tier you can actually buy online is an allowance of TIN verifications. The moment you want the things the homepage leads with — KYB business verification, credit-risk assessment, address verification, Form I-9 and E-Verify, IRS tax transcripts, OFAC and watchlist monitoring — the button changes from Get Started to Contact Sales. So the compliance stack this tool is pitched as has no advertised cost, and the number you can see is the smallest part of it. The second thing to read carefully is that the tiers are allowances rather than rates. You commit to a monthly verification count up front, and the per-match price moves against the smallest buyers. It is $0.79 a check on the cheapest month-to-month plan versus $0.54 on the largest annual one — a 46% spread that falls the wrong way for anyone testing the product at low volume. If your usage is lumpy — a filing-season spike and eleven quiet months — an allowance you buy every month is the wrong shape, and the prepaid option the page offers as a third toggle is worth asking about before you subscribe.

Consensus

Is Consensus worth it?

Consensus is genuinely best for researchers, clinicians, students and writers who want evidence-backed answers drawn from peer-reviewed papers instead of the open web. Skip it if your questions are practical/commercial rather than research-based (a general AI assistant fits), or you need full systematic-review rigor (this accelerates it, not replaces it).

How much does Consensus cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-17, with a caveat) Consensus blocks plain fetches of its pricing page and neither our July nor our August 2026 capture retrieved an amount, so today's figures are not independently confirmed. Our dated reading: a free tier with essential search; Pro about $15/mo, or roughly $10/mo billed annually, with unlimited Pro searches and 15 Deep searches; a Deep tier adding around 200 Deep searches for power users; Teams and Enterprise quoted. The unit that matters is Deep searches rather than seats — that is what the tiers ration. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Consensus?

It searches peer-reviewed literature, which is its strength and its boundary: it can tell you what published studies found, not whether those studies are good. Study quality, sample sizes and conflicting results still need your judgment, and the AI summary layer can smooth over genuine scientific disagreement. Treat it as a dramatically faster literature scan, not an oracle; click through to the papers that matter.

How much does Contractor Foreman cost?

(re-verified 28 Aug 2026 on their own pricing page; all plan prices unchanged since 1 Aug 2026) Priced for the company rather than per seat, with unlimited projects on every tier, and every plan opens with 30 days free before the first bill. Annual: Basic $49/month ($588 a year, 1 user), Standard $105 ($1,264, 3 users), Plus $166 ($1,997, 8 users), Pro $221 ($2,654, 15 users), Unlimited $332 ($3,984, unlimited users). Quarterly costs more for the same plans: Standard $132/month ($395 a quarter), Plus $206 ($619), Pro $282 ($845), Unlimited $415 ($1,245). Basic is not offered quarterly. Their own wording: the fee listed is the fee for the company as a whole. Card payments taken through the built-in Stripe integration are billed separately at 2.9% + $0.30 a transaction (3.9% + $0.30 for Amex, 1% + $0.30 capped at $10.00 for ACH). Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Contractor Foreman?

Credit where it is due first: this is company pricing with unlimited projects and no per-user overage inside your tier, which is rare in construction software and genuinely cheaper than per-seat rivals for a crew. The catch is the billing term. Quarterly costs 26% more than annual on the same plan — $132 against $105 on Standard — and Basic is not sold quarterly at all, so the cheapest way in requires a year paid up front. The user counts are the real ladder: 1, 3, 8, 15, unlimited. A five-person crew is not on Standard at $105, it is on Plus at $166, so count your licence holders before you compare against anything else.

Is Credit Repair Cloud worth it?

Credit Repair Cloud is genuinely best for entrepreneurs building or running a credit-repair business who need client management, dispute automation and done-for-you letter workflows. Skip it if you just want to fix your own personal credit (you don't need this), or you're not prepared for the sales and compliance work a client business demands.

How much does Credit Repair Cloud cost?

(price re-verified 2026-08-21 against the vendor's own pricing page, and now with the published limits rather than approximations) There are five plans, priced monthly. Personal is $49 for 1 user and up to 3 friends and family, and Start $179 for up to 3 users and 300 active clients. Grow is $299 for 6 users and 600 clients, and Scale $399 for 12 users and 1,200 clients. Enterprise is $599 for 24 users and 2,400 clients. Annual billing takes 20% off and is shown as a monthly equivalent on the four business tiers: $143.20, $239.20, $319.20 and $479.20. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Credit Repair Cloud?

This is business-in-a-box software for starting a credit-repair company, not a tool that repairs your own credit — the price only makes sense if you're actually building a client business on it. The middle tiers jump fast as you add clients and seats, and success depends far more on your sales and compliance discipline (credit repair is a regulated space) than on the software. Buy it as an operator, not a consumer.

CrowdStrike

Is CrowdStrike worth it?

CrowdStrike is genuinely best for small and mid-size companies that want a serious endpoint agent without running a security team — Falcon Go is genuinely priced for businesses under 100 devices. Skip it if you are under ten devices and already run a maintained operating system with its built-in protection, because at that size the management overhead outweighs the gain; also skip Go if you actually need EDR, since that starts a tier up.

How much does CrowdStrike cost?

(2026-08-28, taken from the bundle table on their pricing page; the three published per-device rates have not moved since 1 Aug 2026) Priced per device. Falcon Go $7.99 per device per month, or $59.99 per device billed annually — capped at a maximum of 100 devices. Falcon Pro $14.99 monthly or $99.99 annually per device. Falcon Enterprise $19.99 per device per month, or $184.99 per device billed annually — a rate the 1 Aug reading recorded as unpublished. Larger tiers and managed services are quote-only. The free trial is a single 15-day Falcon Free Trial covering Next-Gen Antivirus, Device Control and Express Support, not a trial of each paid tier. Annual subscriptions are billed in full at the start of the term and can be cancelled within 30 days for a full refund. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with CrowdStrike?

Two numbers decide whether this is for you, and neither is the headline. The first is the 100-device ceiling on Falcon Go: it is not a soft limit you grow through, it is the point where you change product. The second is the annual rate, which is worth doing the division on — $59.99 a year against $7.99 a month is $95.88, so annual saves 37%, one of the larger annual discounts in security software. Beyond that, be honest with yourself about which tier you are comparing: Go is antivirus with a good agent, and the endpoint detection and response that CrowdStrike is famous for starts at Pro. Comparing Go against a full EDR product from a competitor is comparing the wrong things.

Databox

Is Databox worth it?

Databox is genuinely best for SMBs and agencies that want clean KPI dashboards from 130+ one-click integrations without building them by hand. Skip it if you need more than the three free sources but only a handful — the next step is Analyst at $71 a month for five — or you want deep custom data modelling, where a free tool like Looker Studio fits better.

How much does Databox cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-12, read on their own pricing page) Databox renamed and repriced its whole ladder since our August reading, so the Analyst/Pro/Growth tiers we described are gone. Free is $0 forever: 1 user, 3 data sources, 1 dashboard, 10 custom metrics, 50 AI credits a month, daily sync. Billed annually: Analyst $71/mo (1 user, 5 sources, unlimited dashboards and reports, 150 credits, hourly sync), Team Core $199/mo (3 users, 10 sources, 500 credits), Team Scale $319/mo (10 users, 30 sources, 1,000 credits, sub-accounts) and Agency from $79/mo (unlimited users, 20 sources, 300 credits), plus a quoted Custom tier. Monthly those read $249, $399 and from $99; annual billing is 20% off. Databox states it does not charge per seat. Extra data sources no longer carry a per-source fee: each plan includes a fixed number and more means moving up a tier, except on Agency, which adds client packs of 1 client, 5 sources and 50 credits for $20/mo annually ($25 monthly). Add-ons are priced separately: branding and white-labeling $200/mo, advanced security management $80/mo, OKRs $160/mo, fiscal calendar $40/mo, 15-minute sync $14/mo per source, and flexible AI credit top-ups. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Databox?

The pricing model bites, just not where it used to. The per-source fee is gone, and for some buyers that is worse rather than better: each plan includes a fixed number of data sources and going over means moving up a whole tier, so one extra integration can mean the $199 Team Core to $319 Team Scale step rather than $5.60. Only the Agency plan sells capacity piecemeal, in $20 client packs. AI credits are a shared monthly pool for Genie and the MCP server, so heavy AI use moves you up a tier as fast as data does, and 15-minute sync — once part of the top plan — is now a $14 per-source add-on on top of the subscription. Power users may also find the custom-metric and data-modelling depth limited next to a full BI tool.

Datarails

Is Datarails worth it?

Datarails is genuinely best for Excel-bound finance teams that have outgrown the spreadsheet plumbing but not the spreadsheets — consolidating several entities or data sources into one governed set of numbers, with planning, reporting and dashboards on top, while the models stay in Excel. Skip it if you are a single-entity company whose budget lives in one workbook, or a team of two that just needs a dashboard — the quote-only sales motion and the tier caps cost more in elapsed time than the problem is worth at that size.

How much does Datarails cost?

(read 2026-08-25 on www.datarails.com/pricing) There is no public price. Datarails publishes three plans — Professional, Premium and Expert — and every one of them carries a Request Quote button where the number would be. The page frames that as a feature: “Get a custom quote tailored to your needs. Complete cost transparency, with no consultant fees—ever.” What the page does publish is the shape of the ladder, and it is unusually explicit about what changes between tiers. The comparison table lists the same feature set on all three plans: reporting, planning, dashboards, Data Mapper, Lookups, Workflows, Submit Button, File Boxes, Filters, version control, the Datarails AI Suite, the FinanceOS AI Connector and permissions. Only two rows differ. Professional gives 1 integration and 2 users with standard support. Premium, marked most popular, gives 2 integrations and 5 users with premium support, and adds AI Storyboards, described as board decks in one click. Expert gives 3 integrations and 15 users with premium support, plus one “additional product included” — the line beyond FP&A runs to Datarails Month-End Close, Datarails Cash, Datarails Spend Control and Datarails Desk, and the page does not say which of those the extra slot may be spent on. So the meter is seats and connected systems, not features. The page states no currency, no billing period, no minimum term and does not say whether a quote is annual, and there is no free plan, no free trial and no self-serve signup: the only entry point on every page of the site is Request Demo or Request Quote. Treat any Datarails figure you have seen quoted elsewhere as unverified — on 25 August 2026 the vendor's own pricing page contains no amount at all. One of those unpublished terms the vendor will answer if you ask. On 31 August 2026, through its partner team, Datarails told us that customers typically commit to an annual term, that billing can run monthly across that year, that users can be added as a team grows, and that adding modules or capabilities beyond the core platform can carry an extra cost that depends on the scope of the expansion. That is the vendor's own account rather than a published rate, so it belongs in your quote in writing — but it does answer the term question the pricing page leaves open. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Datarails?

You cannot find out what it costs without talking to sales, and the entry plan is smaller than it looks: Professional covers 2 users and 1 integration, so a finance team of three, or one that needs both the ERP and the CRM connected, is priced into the next tier before the conversation starts. There is no free plan, no free trial and no self-serve signup anywhere on the site — every path ends at Request Demo — so there is no way to try it quietly before procurement is involved.

Deel

Is Deel worth it?

Deel is genuinely best for companies hiring internationally without local entities — contractors paid compliantly in 150+ countries, or full EOR employment where you need someone on the ground fast. Skip it if your team is all-domestic (a local payroll tool is far cheaper), or you hire enough people in one country that opening your own entity beats paying per-employee EOR fees forever.

How much does Deel cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-17) Pricing is per worker and demo-led, and each product is priced separately. HR and hiring tools start at $14 per worker a month. Contractor management is $49 per contractor a month, or $325 per month per Contractor of Record if you want Deel to carry the compliance risk. US PEO starts at $125 per employee a month. And EOR, where Deel employs your international hire, starts at $599 per employee a month. There is a free demo and final quotes come through sales. The spread is the point: the same headcount costs $14 or $599 a month depending on which relationship you need, so identify the employment model before comparing vendors. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Deel?

Every price is a ‘starting at’ and every button says ‘book a demo’ — the real bill depends on countries, benefits and add-ons, and only firms up in a sales conversation. And know what EOR money buys: at $599+/month per employee you’re paying for compliance risk transfer, not software — worth it in unfamiliar jurisdictions, expensive where you could open your own entity.

Deputy

Is Deputy worth it?

Deputy is genuinely best for shift-based businesses — hospitality, retail, healthcare, any hourly workforce — that need rostering, clocking and labour-law compliance in one system and have enough staff to clear the minimum spend. Skip it if you run a small salaried desk team: the $30 monthly minimum means you pay for roughly six users whether you have them or not, and salaried staff rarely need shift-level clocking at all.

How much does Deputy cost?

(re-read 2026-09-19 on deputy.com/pricing; amounts unchanged since the browser reading of 2026-07-31, which used both billing toggles) Three plans, per user per month and excluding tax. On annual billing Lite is $5, Core $6.50 and Pro $9; switch the toggle to monthly and the same three read $5.50, $7.25 and $10. Add-ons are billed monthly on top. HR is $2 per user, Messaging+ $1.95 per user and Analytics+ $1.50 per user, and the last two are included in Pro. Deputy Payroll enabled by Paycor is $8 per user plus a $49 monthly base fee, available only on annual Core and Pro plans in the US. The page also states a minimum monthly spend of USD $30 / AUD $30 / GBP £20 per invoice on the monthly Lite, Core and Pro plans. Lite and Core start with a free trial; Pro is Contact us rather than self-serve. Two things the page added since: every plan card now carries Deputy AI marked INTRODUCTORY FREE with the line that price and availability may change, so the AI is not a paid-for feature you can hold them to. And a banner across the top offers three months free to businesses that add workforce management and Payroll before 30 November — Payroll being the $8-per-user add-on with the $49 monthly base fee, US-only and annual Core or Pro, so the free months arrive attached to the most expensive thing on the page. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Deputy?

The minimum monthly spend is the number to check before anything else: at $5.50 per user on monthly billing, a team of fewer than about six people still pays the $30 floor, so the headline per-user price is misleading at small scale. Beyond that, the plan you probably want is not the one you first price — auto-scheduling, demand forecasting and labour budgets sit in Core, and SSO, custom access levels and location hierarchies only in Pro, which has no public self-serve checkout. The add-ons stack per user as well, and the payroll add-on is US-only and annual-only.

Descript

Is Descript worth it?

Descript is genuinely best for podcasters and video creators who want to edit by editing the transcript, cutting, overdubbing and stripping filler words as easily as editing a doc. It also suits teams who want those same edits triggered from code or from an AI assistant via Descript’s API and MCP connector rather than from the app. Skip it if you need frame-precise, effects-heavy editing (a traditional NLE fits better) or your monthly volume blows past the media-hour caps.

How much does Descript cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-17) Free plan with 60 minutes a month and watermarked 720p exports. Paid, per person per month, annual against monthly: Hobbyist $16 / $24, Creator $24 / $35, Business $50 / $65, with annual billing saving up to 35%. Media hours and AI credits are capped per tier: roughly 10, 30 and 40 hours a month, and 400 to 1,500 credits. So the meter that matters is footage rather than seats — a weekly podcast can outgrow Hobbyist on hours long before it needs the features above it. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Descript?

It's per-editor pricing with two meters running underneath: media hours AND monthly AI credits. Heavy podcast or video volume burns through the caps fast, and the free tier watermarks 720p exports — model your real production hours before picking a tier. The API changes the arithmetic rather than escaping it. Their own API page states access is included for paying users at no additional cost, but the work it does draws from the same AI credits and media minutes — so automating a pipeline burns the caps faster, not slower. It also stops just short of the finish — the same page says direct media file exports are not supported yet, so the export step stays a manual one in the app. (read on their own API page, 2026-08-14)
