Devs.ai

Is Devs.ai worth it?

Devs.ai is genuinely best for teams that want to prototype and ship AI agents and apps across multiple models without engineering overhead, and that can absorb model usage metered on top of the per-seat fee. Skip it if you need SSO, access control lists or white-labelling without going through an Enterprise contract, or you need one predictable all-in bill rather than a seat fee plus metered usage.

How much does Devs.ai cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-26, read in a live browser session on devs.ai/home/pricing with both billing toggles) Devs.ai does publish its pricing — on the marketing site at devs.ai/home/pricing, not at the app's /pricing route, which bounces you to a login screen. Free is $0 forever, but Agent Builder and App Builder appear there in "Core" rather than "Full" form, data upload is capped at 2 GB, and workflows, APIs and integrations are marked absent. Personal is $15 per seat / month billed annually or $18 billed monthly; Business is $30 per seat / month annually or $36 monthly; Enterprise is a custom annual contract. The seat fee is not the whole bill: Personal includes $10 of usage per month and Business $21, with model usage metered on top of the seat. Data upload runs 2 GB, 10 GB, 20 GB and custom across the four tiers. Team governance and collaboration start at Business, while SSO (OpenID/SAML), access control lists, custom connectors and white-labelling are Enterprise-only. Separately, Devs.ai told us by email on 5 August 2026 that organisations upgrading to a paid Personal or Business plan can earn up to $100 in usage credits for completing setup steps: starting a conversation, creating an agent, connecting an integration, deploying an app. The credits are shared org-wide and do not expire. Enterprise is excluded, and orgs already on a paid plan before July 2026 are not enrolled by default. That last paragraph is the vendor's own statement to us, not something we found published on the pricing page. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Devs.ai?

The seat price is the smaller half of the bill. Personal includes $10 of usage a month and Business $21, with model usage metered on top of the seat. The plan cards state the included amount without stating the rate you pay once it runs out — which is exactly the number you need to forecast a real workload. The free plan is a demo rather than a working tier: both builders are limited to "Core", and workflows, APIs and integrations are absent entirely, which is much of what would make the platform useful past a first agent. And for a product sold on IT governance, the governance controls are the ones you cannot buy with a card — SSO, access control lists and white-labelling sit on the Enterprise tier, behind a custom annual contract.

Is Dext worth it?

Dext is genuinely best for accountants, bookkeepers and growing businesses drowning in receipts who want fast, accurate capture flowing straight into Xero, QuickBooks or Sage. How much does Dext cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-21, read in a browser on dext.com/en/business/pricing because the price panel renders client-side) Dext has moved off named tiers: you choose a region and a billing period, then slide an allowance. The base of 250 documents a month and 5 users costs $25.21 per month billed annually ($302.50 a year) or $31.50 monthly in the Rest of the World, £30 monthly in the UK, €31.50 in the EU and A$42 in APAC, all excluding tax. Dify

Is Dify worth it?

Dify is genuinely best for technical teams that want to build and ship LLM apps and agents on a source-available platform they can self-host and control. Skip it if you want a no-maintenance, non-technical builder — a fully managed no-code AI tool saves you the infra and upkeep.

How much does Dify cost?

Source-available and free of a software fee to self-host — the Community Edition ships under the Dify Open Source License, a modified Apache 2.0 with additional conditions — while the managed Dify Cloud adds a free Sandbox, Professional at $59/mo ($590/yr) and Team at $159/mo ($1,590/yr), each priced per workspace (2026). Self-hosting still costs you infrastructure, model usage and any commercial licence those terms require. Cloud plans include message credits for supported model providers; once those run out you connect your own provider API key and pay that provider directly. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Dify?

'Free' self-hosting isn't free — you own the servers, updates and reliability, and model usage bills separately once the included message credits run out. Dify Cloud takes that infrastructure work off your hands, but every cloud plan is a single workspace, and multiple workspaces, SSO and a commercial licence sit in quote-only Enterprise. It's powerful, but self-hosting it well needs technical comfort.

Digit

Is Digit worth it?

Digit is genuinely best for manufacturers and distributors outgrowing spreadsheets who need inventory, production, purchasing and sales in one system, and who genuinely need lot tracking and traceability rather than just wanting them. Skip it if you are a small operation where $400/month for five users is heavy against what a warehouse module in your existing accounting package would cost; also skip it if your integration list is long, because the per-connection model punishes exactly that — $100 to $125 a month for every integration past the second, depending on which part of their pricing page you read.

How much does Digit cost?

(re-read 2026-09-17 in a rendered browser on heysutton.com/pricing, the rebranded site; unchanged from the 5 September reading on digit-software.com) Four tiers with three published: Starter $400/month including 5 users, Growth $750 including 10, Professional $1,300 including 15, and Enterprise on quote. Extra users are $63/month each on every tier. Paying quarterly takes 10% off — $360, $675 and $1,170 a month, shown with the quarter total beside it — and annually 20%, which is $320, $600 and $1,040 with the year total beside it; the page now opens on Monthly, so the first figure you see is the real monthly rate. Two integrations are included on every plan. The third and each one after it is where the page disagrees with itself: the Integrations block says $100 a month, the FAQ on the same page says $125, and the add-on cards side with the FAQ — API access $125 a month, MCP access $125, push notifications and webhooks $63, all the same price on Starter, Growth and Professional. Customer portals are $18.75 per portal per month on every tier, Enterprise included. We asked Digit which of the two they bill on 5 September 2026 and had no answer; twelve days later, on the rebranded site, both figures were still there. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Digit?

The tier price is a floor, not a ceiling, and the add-ons are where an inventory system quietly doubles. Two integrations are included — but a working setup usually means an accounting package and a webshop, which is your two, and then Amazon or a second sales channel is another $125 a month by the FAQ (the integrations block on the same page still says $100; we have asked which one bills). Want the API, or your data inside an AI assistant? $125 a month each. A Starter customer on QuickBooks, Shopify and Amazon with API access is at $650 a month on the FAQ's numbers, $625 on the block's, before adding a sixth user. Price the whole configuration you actually need, not the tier.

Disputifier

Is Disputifier worth it?

Disputifier is genuinely best for Shopify stores with a chargeback problem big enough to cost real money and nobody in-house to fight disputes, who would rather pay a share of what is recovered than a monthly fee. Skip it if your dispute volume is low or lumpy, you need a predictable monthly bill, or your margins are high enough that auto-refunding an alerted order costs more than losing the occasional dispute.

How much does Disputifier cost?

(read 2026-08-19 and re-verified 2026-09-17 on apps.shopify.com/disputifier and disputifier.com/pricing) Free to install, with everything billed on use, and unchanged between those two readings. The listing shows a single plan — Basic, free to install, with a 7-day risk-free trial — and names two charges: chargeback fighting at 20% of won-back revenue, and chargeback alerts from $17 to $27.50 per alert. All charges are billed in USD. The vendor's own pricing page fills in the rest. Recovery is 20% of the recovered amount with the fee capped at $250 per win — win a $50 chargeback and they debit $10 — while fraud prevention is $0.05 per order and 'order not received' prevention another $0.05 per order. Alerts are sold there as custom volume-based pricing by quote, so the $17-$27.50 band on the listing is the only public number for them. There is no monthly subscription anywhere in this: the bill is a function of how many disputes and orders you have, so it cannot be read off the pricing page in advance. Developer Disputifier, 294 Trillium Way, Clayton, North Carolina; the app launched 23 September 2020 and carries a 4.6 average over 125 App Store reviews. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Disputifier?

Free to install is doing a lot of work here. All four services bill per event — a fifth of each recovery, $17 to $27.50 an alert, five cents an order twice over — so the cost lands after the fact and scales with exactly the thing you are trying to reduce. Model your worst chargeback month before you switch alerts on, not your average one. The reviews deserve a straight look too. The 4.6 average across 125 reviews, read again on 17 September 2026, is not a middle: 87% are five stars and twelve of the one hundred and twenty-five are a single star, with almost nothing in between. That shape has held while the count grew — on 22 August the same listing showed 4.6 from 88 reviews with nine one-stars, so both ends have filled at roughly the same rate. The most recent one-star we read, from June 2026, describes a security incident at the vendor and a dispute over unpaid invoices and billing descriptors; Disputifier replied in July that it does not block merchants from moving providers and disputes the account of the charges. We have not verified either side and are not taking one. What the pattern does show is where the friction sits: merchants either rate this perfect or fall out with it over invoices, not over whether disputes were won. And know what alerts cannot reach. Coverage depends on the shopper's bank and card: smaller issuers do not participate, Amex and Discover frequently cannot be set up, Klarna alerts do not exist yet and PayPal alerts are recent. The prevention rate the vendor quotes is a ceiling for a well-covered card mix, not a promise for yours.

EasyDMARC

Is EasyDMARC worth it?

EasyDMARC is genuinely best for anyone who sends email from their own domain and has not yet got DMARC to enforcement — the reporting is the part that makes that safe rather than guesswork. Skip it if you have one domain, send little email, and are willing to read raw XML reports yourself, because the free tier plus patience covers the same ground.

How much does EasyDMARC cost?

(re-read 2026-09-17 on easydmarc.com/pricing, which now redirects to /pricing/easydmarc/businesses; every amount and every plan limit below is unchanged since 26 August 2026) Plus is 44.99 a month, or 35.99 billed annually, for 2 domains, three months of report history and one invited user. Premium is 89.99, or 71.99 annually, for 4 domains, a year of history, TLS reports, EasySPF, alert management and unlimited invited users, with a dedicated CS manager on the yearly plan. Enterprise is quoted and carries three years of history, unlimited users and domains, managed services, a dedicated DMARC engineer, SSO, audit logs, reputation monitoring and API access. Annual billing is marked as 20% off plus bonus features, and MSPs and MSSPs get a separate tab with their own ladder. The number that actually sets your tier is monthly email volume: each card has its own selector, running 100,000 to 1,000,000 messages on Plus and up to 5,000,000 on Premium, so the headline is the floor for the smallest bracket. Prices are exclusive of taxes and taxes may be applied at checkout. On the currency: read from the Netherlands the page prints these as euro amounts and read over a US connection as the identical dollar figures — €44.99 there is $44.99 here, so the symbol changes and the number does not. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with EasyDMARC?

You are buying domains and history, not features. Plus covers two domains; a company with a main domain, a marketing subdomain and a legacy domain it forgot about is already over. And the history window is the part that bites later: three months on Plus means that when you are investigating a spoofing campaign that started in the spring, the reports are gone. The page also does not say what happens when you pass the message volume. Their own FAQ does: you lose access to your reports and dashboard rather than being billed for overage. That is a harsher failure mode than an overage charge, and worth knowing before you size the plan.

ElevateForward

Is ElevateForward worth it?

ElevateForward is genuinely best for scaling companies where growth is outpacing alignment — leadership lacks execution visibility and strategic initiatives keep stalling halfway. Skip it if you already have solid execution discipline, or you just want a simple task or project tool — this is a strategy-and-alignment layer, not a to-do list.

How much does ElevateForward cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-21, read on their own pricing page) The prices are published, despite the consultative first impression, and the report figures have not moved since our 12 August reading. An Elevate Insight Report is $500, a bundle of three is $1,350 at a 10% discount, and the Elevate Studio tiers render at $250, $500 and $750 a month. Those three are Elevate Strategy, Elevate Execution and Business Enablement, the last still marked coming soon. A report is scoped to one focus area you choose, delivered within five business days of submission, with up to two rounds of feedback-based adjustments and a review consultation included. Treat the monthly figures as provisional: each price sits in a pair of spans whose second value reads $1,500 per report. For the three monthly tiers those second values are $8, $16 and $149. The page carries no visible label for either state, so which figure a given buyer is shown cannot be settled from the markup. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with ElevateForward?

It's a strategy-to-execution layer, not a task app or a dashboard — so the value only lands if leadership actually adopts the framework. Emergent

Is Emergent worth it?

Emergent is genuinely best for founders, PMs and builders who want to turn a prompt into a working full-stack web app or MVP fast, without writing the code themselves. Skip it if you need a hardened, security-audited production system with no human review, or your usage would burn through credits faster than the value returns.

How much does Emergent cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-31 on emergent.sh/pricing) Free $0 (10 credits/mo); Standard $20/mo, or $17/mo billed annually, for 100 credits (the page states a $36 annual saving, 15%); Pro $200/mo, or $167/mo billed annually, for 750 credits (a $396 saving, 16.5%). Above Pro the ladder is quote-only: Business and Enterprise both show 'Custom' and route to Book a demo, adding RBAC, SSO, shared workspaces and co-editing (Business) and user-level credit limits, audit logs, self-hosted database support, VPC deployment and priority SLAs (Enterprise). The $300/mo Team tier with 1,250 shared credits is no longer offered. Credits meter the work -- simple changes 1-2, medium features 5-10, complex refactors 10-20+ -- which comes from earlier research and was not re-confirmed on the 31 Aug read of the pricing page. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Emergent?

The real cost is credit consumption, not the flat sticker -- a genuinely complex app burns 10-20+ credits per big change, so serious builders move up tiers faster than the price list implies. Now do the division, because Emergent's own pricing page doesn't. Standard is $20 for 100 credits, so 20 cents a credit. Pro is $200 for 750, which is 26.7 cents. Moving up a tier makes every unit of work more expensive, not less: Pro costs 33% more per credit than Standard, and annual billing keeps the gap (17 cents against 22.3). That is the opposite of the volume discount the ladder looks like -- you are buying a 1M-token context window, custom agents and priority support, not cheaper work. And there is nowhere cheap to go above it: Business and Enterprise are both quote-only, so passing 750 credits a month means a sales call rather than a bigger plan. Check what your month actually needs before you upgrade; one Standard month plus purchased extra credits may beat one Pro month. AI-generated code still needs a human eye: review it for security, correctness and maintainability before anything touches real users.

Employment Hero

Is Employment Hero worth it?

Employment Hero is genuinely best for Australian and New Zealand employers who want HR and payroll from one vendor and will genuinely use both, where the per-employee rate beats separate systems. Skip it if you are budgeting in US dollars — the page is Australian, in AUD excluding GST, with no US equivalent — or you only need one of the modules, since each is sold separately and priced as though you are buying the platform.

How much does Employment Hero cost?

(2026-08-28, read on their own pricing page at employmenthero.com/pricing/) EVERY PRICE ON THAT PAGE IS AUD EXCLUDING GST — the page states it in its own disclaimer. It is the Australian page; separate pages exist for the UK, New Zealand, Singapore, Malaysia and Canada, and there is no US one. Sold as separate modules rather than one plan. Recruitment: a free ATS tier at A$0 that includes 50 complimentary AI video interviews as a one-off trial, then A$199, A$359 and A$639 a month for 50, 100 and 200 interviews. The disclaimer also names a 300-interview plan but publishes no price for it. Those are annual-billing rates — the disclaimer gives A$2,388 a year for the 50-interview plan at a 20% discount, against A$249 a month on monthly billing. Interview credits reset monthly, unused credits do not roll over, and overage is billed automatically with no hard cap. No HR or payroll subscription is required for Recruitment. HR Essentials A$10 per employee per month, HR Engage A$14; HR Elite and Employment Unlimited are quote-only. There is no standalone payroll tier on the page any more: payroll sits inside Employment Unlimited, and Managed Payroll is an add-on at A$20 per employee per month with a A$400 monthly minimum. Other add-ons, each per employee per month with a stated monthly minimum: Chat A$2 (min A$20), Rostering and Time and Attendance A$4 (A$40), HR Advisory A$14 (A$280), Learning 10-course bundle A$8 (A$80), LMS Plus A$17 (A$170), EAP Standard A$8 (A$80), EAP Premium A$10 (A$100). A minimum of 10 Users applies, billed on the higher of the users named in your agreement or the active users. Every price on the page carries an asterisk. CHANGED SINCE 1 AUG 2026: the standalone Payroll module at $10 per employee recorded then is no longer on the page, and the currency was not recorded as AUD. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Employment Hero?

There is no such thing as the Employment Hero price, and the first thing to fix is the currency: the pricing page is the Australian one, its disclaimer says every figure is AUD excluding GST, and there is no US page. Then the structure. HR, recruitment, chat and payroll are separate subscriptions, most of them per employee, with a stated minimum of ten users underneath — so a six-person company is billed for ten. Payroll no longer carries its own price at all: it is inside the quote-only Employment Unlimited tier, or an A$20-a-head add-on with a A$400 floor. And the recruitment tiers meter AI video interviews rather than people, with credits that reset monthly, do not roll over, and overage billed automatically with no hard cap.

Envoy

Is Envoy worth it?

Envoy is genuinely best for companies with a real office and a front desk problem — visitor compliance, desk booking and deliveries in one system, where the per-location price is spread over enough employees to be worth it. Skip it if you are remote-first or have one small office — a visitor log and a shared calendar cover the same ground, and the per-location floor is priced for buildings rather than teams.

How much does Envoy cost?

(2026-08-29, read on their own pricing page and their platform page) Five products, each metered differently and all billed annually. Visitors: Basic free, Premium $362 per location per month, Enterprise quote-only. Emergency Notifications $2 per user per month. Reservations $5 per bookable resource per month. Screens $12 per device per month. Deliveries $250 per delivery location per month. All five amounts are unchanged since 1 August 2026, and the pricing page now publishes the Visitors Premium rate in JSON-LD with a billingDuration of one year. The line 'Price excludes platform fee' appears exactly four times, on Emergency Notifications, Reservations, Screens and Deliveries — not on the Visitors card. The platform page supplies the shape of that fee without its number: the platform is required to use Envoy products, it is 'priced as a flat annual fee', and it has its own Premium and Enterprise tiers that you choose before adding modules. That page also lists visitor management as one of the modules. Invoicing by ACH is offered only on Enterprise or with three or more Premium Yearly locations. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Envoy?

None of the prices on that page is a price you can budget from. Four of the five product cards are followed by 'Price excludes platform fee', and the platform page says outright that the platform is required to use Envoy products and is charged as a flat annual fee — with its own Premium and Enterprise tiers to pick first, and no number published anywhere on the site. That is the first thing to ask for. The second is how the meters stack, because they are all in different units: per location, per user, per bookable resource, per device, per delivery location. An office with forty bookable desks, six screens and one delivery point is paying on four clocks at once: $362 for the location, $200 for the desks, $250 for deliveries, and $180 for the screens rather than the $72 the card implies, because Envoy's own llms.txt sets a 15-device minimum that the pricing page never mentions. That is $992 a month before the platform fee, and before emergency notifications, which the same file bills at $2 a head across your entire employee directory rather than across the people who use them. Price the combination you actually want, not the product you came for.

ExpertEx

Is ExpertEx worth it?

ExpertEx is genuinely best for online stores that need a steady stream of product photos and short video ads and would otherwise pay a studio or a freelancer per shoot -- the e-commerce studio pushes results straight to the live store on Ultra and above. Skip it if you need one hero shoot a year (a photographer is cheaper and better), your products are hard to render convincingly, or you want a free tier to evaluate on -- generating always costs credits.

How much does ExpertEx cost?

(read 2026-08-22 in a live browser on expertex.ai/pricing -- plain fetches return an empty page, so this is the first reading we have of the real ladder) There are four credit tiers, monthly against yearly with two months free. Starter is $10 or $7 for 1,000 credits, and Premium $30 or $20 for 3,000. Ultra is $75 or $45 for 7,500, and Professional $250 or $149 for 25,000. Paid three-day trials sit alongside them at $1 for Premium (1,000 credits), $2 for Ultra (2,000) and $5 for Professional (5,000), with full access and cancellation before renewal. The page's own calculator prices the work at 25 credits per product photo, 250 per short video ad and 2,000 per full reel, and reels require Ultra or above. Credits roll over month to month while the subscription is active and are lost on cancellation; you can top up on demand or upgrade mid-month, with upgrades prorated immediately and downgrades taking effect at the next billing date. Prompt Builder is free to use before subscribing. One label on that page understates its own offer. The monthly/yearly toggle is sold as two months free, which would be a sixth off, but the yearly rates sit far below that: $7 against $10 is 30% off, $20 against $30 is a third, and $45 against $75 and $149 against $250 are both about 40%. Counted in months rather than percentages, a year costs 8.4 months of Starter, 8 of Premium and 7.2 of Ultra and Professional — so the saving runs from three and a half to nearly five months, not two, and on this page the annual commitment is worth more than the vendor claims for it. ExpertEx told us through PartnerStack on 11 September 2026 that this label and the card-versus-calculator gap are both with their website team as a priority, so expect these figures to move. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with ExpertEx?

The plan cards and the calculator on the same page do not agree, and the gap is large enough to pick the wrong tier. Starter is sold as 65 product photos and 10 short video ads a month on 1,000 credits; at the calculator's own rates that work costs 4,125 credits, four times the allowance. The card numbers only reconcile if each is taken alone, and at roughly 15 credits a photo and 100 a video. The same 4.2x mismatch repeats on every tier, so it is a systematic difference rather than a typo. Ask which rate governs before you buy, because the calculator will steer you to a plan two rungs above what the card implies. The second thing to price is that this is a credit product with no free tier. Prompt Builder is free; generating is not. And the cheap trials ($1, $2, $5) are three days rather than a month -- enough to test output quality, not enough to test a monthly workflow. And the fundamental one: AI product photography is judged on whether the output is publishable without a retoucher. Run your own worst product through the trial -- a reflective bottle, a garment with fine texture -- rather than the kind of thing that looks good in every demo.

Strong for HubSpot-centric B2B teams; less compelling if you're not in that ecosystem.

How much does Fastmail cost?

What is the catch with Fastmail?

Is Filevine worth it?

Filevine is genuinely best for law firms past the point where a general project tool works, with enough matters that case-specific workflows and intake tracking pay back a negotiated annual contract. Skip it if you are a small practice wanting a published price to compare — this category will not give you one, and simpler practice-management tools do publish theirs.

How much does Filevine cost?

(2026-08-29, read on their own pricing page) No prices are shown. Their own wording: all packages are custom built for your team's needs. The page describes five products — LOIS (AI features), Matters, Intake, Depositions and Signatures — and routes everything to a sales team for a custom quote. No per-user cost, no minimum seats, no contract length and no implementation fee is published, which is unchanged since 1 August 2026. What is the catch with Filevine?

Nothing is priced, so everything is negotiable — which cuts both ways. In legal software the number that usually surprises people is not the per-seat rate but implementation: data migration from your existing case system, template configuration and training, quoted separately and often as a one-off in the thousands. That figure is absent here along with everything else. Ask for it as its own line, ask what the contract length is, and ask what happens to your matter data if you leave. A custom quote is fine; a custom quote you cannot compare against a rate card is what you are trying to avoid.

Flatpay

Is Flatpay worth it?

Flatpay is genuinely best for a physical shop, café or bar with steady card turnover above £995 a month that wants one predictable rate on every card type instead of a pricing table full of card categories. The on-site installation and 24/7 support suit an owner who would rather not configure hardware, and daily payouts help if cash flow is tight. Skip it if your card takings are small or seasonal: the £995 monthly minimum and the £39 shortfall charge punish exactly that pattern, and the price list says the assessment is annual so a quiet winter still counts. Skip it too if you are online-only — this is built around physical terminals and an in-person sales visit — or if you turn over more than £200,000 a year on cards, because then every published price is replaced by a quote.

How much does Flatpay cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-13 on flatpay.com/en-gb/pricing and their separate /pricelist page; every amount unchanged since 2026-08-13) Flatpay charges one rate per transaction on every card type, with no monthly subscription. The card terminal is 1.49% and costs GBP 0 to start. The three point-of-sale tiers all run at 1.69% and differ only in what you pay up front for onboarding and installation: GBP 995 for mobile POS, GBP 1,495 for POS Pro and GBP 2,495 for POS Premium. Every one of those four prices is labelled "Below GBP 200,000 in annual card turnover". CHANGED SINCE AUGUST: the above-threshold panel is no longer reachable on the UK page -- there is no visible toggle, and the markup for it sits in a display:none component variant. That hidden markup holds both a "custom rate - contact sales" variant and a "0,99% / EUR 0" variant; neither renders for a UK visitor, and the comma decimal and euro sign mark the second as another market's card, so we do not quote 0.99% as a Flatpay UK rate (a WebFetch read it as a live figure on 2026-08-20 and that was wrong then too). Included on all plans: 24/7 support, daily payouts and a customer portal. The part that needs reading is the price list, linked from the footer rather than from the pricing page. It sets a minimum monthly turnover of GBP 995 and charges GBP 39 in any month you fall short -- assessed annually, so seasonal businesses are explicitly in scope. It also lists GBP 410 for a replacement terminal, GBP 25 to re-onboard after a change in business registration, and hardware separately: tablet GBP 495, printer GBP 495, cash drawer GBP 200. All of those figures exclude VAT. One inconsistency worth raising with their salesperson: the pricing page puts POS Pro onboarding at GBP 1,495 while the price list quotes a "Pro POS bundle" at GBP 1,995 -- a GBP 500 gap between two pages on the same site. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Flatpay?

“Zero monthly fees” is the headline, and there genuinely is no subscription — but there is a floor. Miss £995 of card turnover in a month and you are charged £39, which for a quiet month is the same thing as a monthly fee wearing a different hat. Do the sum before you sign: a shop turning over £500 on cards pays £7.45 in transaction fees and then £39 on top, so the cost of taking cards is six times what the rate suggests. The rate itself is straightforward and the terminal really is free to start, so this is a good fit for a busy till and a poor one for a market stall, a seasonal business or anyone whose card volume swings. And the four-figure POS installation charges are real money that the “no hidden fees” promise sits directly above — they are disclosed, but they are not what most people picture when they read that line.

Flippa

Is Flippa worth it?

Flippa is genuinely best for buyers and sellers of small-to-mid online businesses, SaaS, apps and domains who want the largest marketplace and are comfortable doing their own due diligence. Skip it if you're selling a large, premium business where a dedicated broker's vetting and negotiation earns its higher fee, or you want a hands-off sale.

How much does Flippa cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-03 on their own pricing page, USD) Sellers pay an upfront listing fee plus a success fee when the business sells, and BOTH change with the asking-price band. The success fee is 10% on every band up to $499.9K, then 9% from $500K, 8% from $1M, 7% from $5M, 6% from $10M and 5% at $50M+. The listing fee is not one figure either: on a sub-$10K asset it is $29 flat for a 60-day term, $49 flat for a boosted three-month listing, or $199 per six months for Premium; from $10K the ladder is $49, $399 or $599 per six months; from $50K it is $129, $399 or $699. From $100K upward there is no self-service option at all — Flippa routes those to its own brokers at a single upfront $399 to $1,499 for a six-month brokerage term, with the success fee still on top. Buyers browse free — AI matching, Deal Room, FlippaPay, escrow and multi-currency are all included — and a paid buyer subscription ($49 a month or $490 a year, up from $388 a year in our July and August 2026 captures) adds 21 days of exclusive early access to new listings valued over $10,000. Payment charges are published too: FlippaPay from 1% and Escrow.com from 1.2%. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Flippa?

The real cost is the success fee, and it's a flat bracket rate on the whole sale price, not marginal — a mid-five-figure sale can hand over a four- to five-figure commission. Flippa is also a self-serve marketplace: more listings, but more due-diligence burden on you. Verification and quality vary, so buyers must vet hard and sellers should expect tyre-kickers. It's reach and liquidity, not a white-glove broker.

Is Foxit worth it?

Foxit is genuinely best for individuals and businesses that want full PDF editing, OCR and organizing at a fraction of Acrobat's subscription — especially multi-seat deployments. Skip it if you only read and occasionally annotate PDFs (free readers cover it), or your org is contractually deep in the Adobe ecosystem.

How much does Foxit cost?

(re-verified 1 Sep 2026 on the Foxit pricing pages and the eSign JSON-LD) PDF Editor is $129.99 per user per year, or from $10.99 per user per month, with a 14-day free trial that asks for no card. PDF Editor+ is $159.99 per user per year and bundles what used to be a second bill: mobile apps, AI Smart Redact, cloud storage and Foxit eSign with 150 envelopes a year. A perpetual licence, Foxit PDF Editor 14, is $209.99 once per user for Windows or macOS, covering editing, conversion, OCR, forms and security but not the AI Assistant, Smart Redact, the document management system or any cloud feature. Every Editor seat carries 300 free AI credits per user per month; the paid AI Assistant tier is $49.99 a year for 2,000 credits a month per user, credits reset monthly and do not roll over, and the add-on is not sold for perpetual licences. Standalone eSign still exists and the pricing page prints it in its JSON-LD: Essentials is $120.00 a year with 250 envelopes and 10 templates for one dedicated user, Business is $300.00 per licence a year with unlimited envelopes and a five-licence minimum, so $1,500 is the smallest Business order; volume licensing starts at 30 licences and is quoted. Foxit publishes a volume discount of up to 30% for larger teams. An education discount is applied automatically at checkout when you register with a school email address; Foxit does not publish the rate. Foxit states the comparison figures are US list rates. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Foxit?

Foxit's pitch is being the honest fraction of Acrobat's price, and on the headline numbers it delivers. The catch used to be that e-signing was always a second subscription. As of our 28 August 2026 reading that is only true below Editor+, which now includes Foxit eSign with 150 envelopes a year. The trap has moved rather than gone: buy the $129.99 Editor, then find you need signatures, and Editor plus standalone eSign Essentials is $249.99 a year against $159.99 for the Editor+ that bundles it. AI is still an add-on at $49.99 a year beyond the 300 monthly credits, and it is not sold for perpetual licences at all. Price the basket, not the first line.

FreshBooks

Is FreshBooks worth it?

FreshBooks is genuinely best for freelancers and small service firms who bill time and want invoicing that chases payment for them — the client cap is generous enough at Plus for most one-person businesses. Skip it if you have more than a handful of clients and only need invoicing — the client cap is what pushes you up the ladder here, and flat-priced invoicing tools do not care how many clients you have.

How much does FreshBooks cost?

(2026-08-29, read on their own pricing page) Every amount is unchanged since 1 August 2026. The prices shown are 90% off for three months. Lite $2.30 a month promotional, $23 regular, 5 billable clients. Plus $4.30 promotional, $43 regular, 50 billable clients. Premium $7 promotional, $70 regular, unlimited clients. Select is quote-only. Team members are $11 a month each on every tier, with 2 included on Select. Two add-ons sit in the fine print: Advanced Payments $20 a month and Payroll $40 a month plus $6 per user. The page prints the promotional saving beside each tier — $62.10 on Lite, $116.10 on Plus, $189.00 on Premium — which is exactly three months of the difference and confirms the discount runs three months and no longer. Separately, there is a 30-day free trial, and FreshBooks promises a full refund if you contact them within 30 days of purchase. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with FreshBooks?

Two things multiply the number you first see. The promotional rate is 90% off for three months, so Lite is $23 rather than $2.30 from month four. And the meter is billable clients, not invoices or revenue: Lite stops at five, which a freelancer with a handful of retainers passes in their first good quarter, and the step to fifty clients is $43. Then people cost extra on top at $11 each, so a two-person studio on Plus is $54 a month rather than $43. Payroll is a separate $40 plus $6 a head, which is the line most people miss when they compare this against a bookkeeping tool that includes it.

Glide

Is Glide worth it?

Glide is genuinely best for teams that already run operations out of spreadsheets and want a real app on top — internal tools, field data capture, client portals — without hiring a developer. Skip it if you need heavy custom logic or a high-write consumer app: the update metering punishes exactly that shape of workload, and anything beyond the published ceilings is a quote-only conversation.

How much does Glide cost?

(Classic re-verified 2026-09-03 in a live browser session on both tabs and both billing toggles, unchanged since 2026-08-04; the GlideOS plans were re-read the same day after a restructure) The classic spreadsheet-to-app builder still has a genuinely free tier: unlimited drafts, one editor, up to 25k rows. Its paid plans are unchanged, but they now live on glideapps.com/classic/pricing instead of the main pricing page. On the individual tab, Explorer starts at $19 per month billed yearly or $25 billed monthly (one app, 100 personal users, 250 updates, up to 25k rows) and Maker at $49 per month billed yearly or $60 monthly. On the business tab, Business starts at $199 per month billed yearly or $249 monthly, with unlimited apps, 30 users included, 5,000 updates and up to 100k rows. Extra users are $5 each on annual billing and $6 on monthly, and extra updates cost 2 cents each. Enterprise is quote-only. VAT is added at checkout, and Business comes with a 14-day free trial. Glide now sells two products from two pages. What glideapps.com/pricing shows today is GlideOS, the newer AI builder, on its own separate subscription, and its plan ladder was rebuilt between our August and September readings. The Free, Solo and Team tiers we recorded on 4 August 2026 are gone; as read on 3 September 2026 the ladder is Free, Basic at $25 per month, Plus at $50, Pro at $125 and a quote-only Enterprise. Free carries 2 team members, 10 projects and limited credits, with no published apps. Basic adds 100 credits, 2 published apps and 1 enabled workflow. Plus gives 250 credits, 3 team members, 5 published apps, 20 projects and 5 workflows, with credit bundles up to 10,000 a month. Pro starts at 5 team members with 500 credits, 50 credits included per member, unlimited published apps and workflows, premium integrations and the option to build with your own agent over MCP. Two things that used to be on the page are not any more: the $10 per additional member above five, and Glide's note that annual GlideOS plans were coming. So the GlideOS prices are still monthly only, and what a sixth seat costs is no longer published. Credit top-ups are capped at one a month on Basic and two on Plus, and are unlimited on Pro and Enterprise. One caveat on the row ceilings, from Glide's own partner team on 18 August 2026. The 25k limit applies to synced spreadsheet sources regardless of plan type, and the 100k+ figures apply to their Big Table data source. So which ceiling you hit is set by where the data lives as much as by what you pay. They also confirmed the Individual and Business tabs on the pricing page are audience segmentation rather than two halves of one plan ladder. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Glide?

Glide meters updates, not just seats. Your bill tracks how busy the app is rather than how many people own a licence. A write-heavy workflow can pass the included 5,000 updates and add 2 cents each without anyone changing plan. The jump from Maker to Business is also steep: $49 to $199 per month on annual billing, and the row ceiling only moves from 25k to 100k. Per Glide's partner team on 18 August 2026, those ceilings track the data source rather than the plan. 25k applies to synced spreadsheet sources whatever you pay, and the 100k+ figures belong to their Big Table source. So an upgrade alone does not lift a spreadsheet-backed app past 25k rows. The pricing page is hard to read for a reason Glide does not dispute. Asked about the Individual and Business tabs, the same team called them audience segmentation. They agreed on 18 August 2026 that the comparison tables confuse, because they never put all plans side by side. And there is a trap before any of that arithmetic applies: everything above describes the classic builder, but the free signup button on glideapps.com does not lead there. We pressed it on 7 September 2026 and landed in GlideOS, the newer AI product, which meters credits instead of updates, starts at $25 a month instead of $199, and describes its free tier only as “limited credits” with no number anywhere in the pricing page or the app. Read the classic prices, click sign up, and you are evaluating something else.

Goflow

Is Goflow worth it?

Goflow is genuinely best for multichannel e-commerce operations with serious order volume that want orders, inventory, purchasing, listings and shipping unified — including EDI retail. Skip it if you sell on one channel only (a single-channel tool costs a tenth), or your volume sits just past the free Core allowance but nowhere near what the $499/mo Launch tier is worth.

How much does Goflow cost?

(re-verified 3 Sep 2026 on goflow.com/pricing; Scale changed, the rest held) Goflow Core is free forever at $0/mo with no credit card, covering 500 orders, 3 users, 1,000 newly created listings and every Goflow feature. Paid tiers add unlimited channels, users, integrations and listings — Launch $499/mo (750 orders), Scale $979/mo (5,000 orders plus wholesale/EDI orders; on 12 Aug this tier was $1,449 for 10,000 orders, so it has been cut in price and in volume), Enterprise custom — and Enterprise is the one annual commitment, twelve months, where every other paid plan is month-to-month. Billing is month-to-month with no setup or implementation fees, but EDI, SFTP and AS2 connections carry a one-time setup and testing fee of typically $500-1,300 per connection. There is no free trial and no discount programme — the free Core plan is the trial (vendor mail, 12 Aug 2026). Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Goflow?

Every tier ships the whole platform — you pay for volume, not unlocks — and Core really is free forever, so 500 orders a month costs $0. The catch is the cliff above it: orders past the Core allowance are paused until you upgrade, and the next step up is $499/mo. Goflow also states you cannot move back down to Core once you go paid. Budget the EDI setup separately: $500-1,300 one-time per connection, multiplied by the number of retail partners.

(2026-08-29, read on their own pricing page) Priced per seat, with both billing columns published. Billed annually: Starter $4.99, Grow $7.50, Team $10, Enterprise $25 per seat per month. Billed monthly: Starter $7, Grow $9, Team $12; Enterprise is sold annually only. The annual column is labelled 'Annual (2 months free)'. Starter, Grow and Team each carry a 2 seat minimum, so the smallest bill Hubstaff will issue is $9.98 a month rather than $4.99. Their FAQ is explicit about what a seat means: 'Hubstaff charges per seat. This means that even if only two out of your three team members are using its tracking features, you will still have to pay for all three members.' Seats added mid-term on an annual plan are prorated — their example is $60 for a seat added in July on a January-to-December plan. Six add-ons are sold per seat per month: Insights, More screenshots, Tasks and Silent app from $2.50, Locations from $3.33, Data retention from $1.67. VIP Support is $29 a month per organisation for accounts spending $275-$400 a month and $59 above $400. There is a 14-day free trial with no credit card, a 30-day money-back guarantee excluding that trial, and a free Project Viewer role for people who only read reports. Re-verified 2026-09-19, reading the plan cards as they render rather than as they ship: every figure above is unchanged — $4.99/$7.50/$10/$25 annual against $7/$9/$12 monthly, the two-seat minimum on the first three tiers, Enterprise annual-only, the six add-on rates, and VIP Support at $29 and $59 per organisation. Two details are worth having: Tasks is included from Grow upward and Locations is not available at all on Starter or Grow, so the add-on menu differs per tier rather than simply costing the same everywhere. The page's plan data still carries a dormant Free tier that does not render as a card and that the FAQ does not acknowledge — asked whether you can use Hubstaff for free, it answers with the 14-day trial. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Hubstaff?

The seat definition is the whole story and they state it plainly, which deserves credit: you pay for every seat you have created, not every person actually being tracked. So an account that grew with your headcount and never got tidied up is billing you for people who left. Audit your seat list before renewal, not after. The second thing is that features on the lower tiers are add-ons rather than inclusions — Insights, Tasks, Silent app and extra screenshots at $2.50 a seat a month, Locations at $3.33 — so the plan you priced and the plan that does what you watched in the demo may differ by ten dollars a seat. And be clear with yourself about why you are buying it: screenshots and activity levels change how a team feels about their work, and that cost does not appear on any pricing page.

IDrive

Is IDrive worth it?

IDrive is genuinely best for individuals and small businesses that want cheap multi-device cloud backup with versioning, snapshots and physical seed/restore shipping. Skip it if you need real-time sync-and-collaborate storage (that's Drive/Dropbox territory, not backup), or you won't tolerate the year-two renewal jump.

How much does IDrive cost?

(2026, re-verified 4 Sep 2026) Free 10 GB Basic tier. IDrive Mini is $0.25/month for 100 GB ($0.83/month for 500 GB). IDrive Personal is $9.99/month for 5 TB, shown discounted to $6.99/month with annual billing (save 30%), scaling up to $99.99/month ($69.99 discounted) at 100 TB. Those are the yearly-billing figures the page opens on; switch it to the Monthly tab and the same Personal 5 TB plan is $11.99/month, and a two-year plan is advertised at up to 50% off the first year. Team starts at the same $9.99/month ($6.99 discounted) for 5 computers and 5 users on 5 TB; Business starts at $19.99/month ($13.99 discounted) for 500 GB with unlimited users. Add-ons: server backup $5/month/server, and Microsoft 365 or Google Workspace backup at $20/seat/year. The honest catch is on the pricing page in IDrive's own words: the discounted price applies to the first year of the selected plan, and standard pricing applies from the second year onward. Two further conditions sit in the same footnotes: going over your storage limit costs $0.25/GB/month on Personal and $0.50/GB/month on Team and Business, and the discount is for new signups only and is not available if you sign up from IDrive's mobile apps. Every figure above read the same on 4 September 2026 as on 27 August 2026. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with IDrive?

The first-year price is a teaser and the renewal is the price — IDrive says so itself under the plan table, and on the current numbers the step from discounted to standard is about 43% (Personal 5 TB goes from $6.99 to $9.99 a month). Budget on year-two rates, not the banner, and note that the discount is for new signups only and not available through their mobile apps. Overrun your storage and there is a further $0.25/GB/month on Personal, $0.50 on Team and Business. Beyond that, the honest backup fundamentals apply: a backup you never test-restore is a hope, not a backup, and the first full backup of multi-TB data takes weeks over the wire unless you use their physical Express seed shipping.

Is InVideo worth it?

How much does InVideo cost?

Four paid tiers plus a free plan, read on invideo.io/pricing on 5 September 2026. On annual billing: Plus $17 a month (750 AI credits a year), Max $85 a month (3,900 credits), Generative $170 a month (8,000 credits) and Elite $900 a month (42,500 credits); month-to-month the same tiers are $20, $100, $200 and $1,000. What is the catch with InVideo?

There is also no free way in any more: on 31 August 2026 the pricing page published no free tier at all, so the cheapest route to trying it is the Plus plan at $20 a month. AI video is fast but generic by default: templated output needs real editing and brand direction to not look like everyone else's. Treat it as a first-draft engine, not a finished-studio replacement.

Is Jibble worth it?

How much does Jibble cost?

(read 2026-08-30 on jibble.io, re-checked 2026-09-19) There is no published price for anything above the free plan. What is the catch with Jibble?

How much does Jotform AI Agents cost?

(re-verified 4 Sep 2026 on jotform.com/ai/agents/pricing, on the yearly tab; the same figures were read from a US exit on 26 Aug 2026. What is the catch with Jotform AI Agents?

It's also tightly bound to the Jotform ecosystem: brilliant if you already live in Jotform forms, less compelling as a standalone chatbot if you don't. For form-driven support and lead capture, it's a fast, low-code win.

Is Katana MRP worth it?

Katana MRP is genuinely best for product makers and small manufacturers who need live inventory, production scheduling and multi-channel order sync in one cloud platform. How much does Katana MRP cost?

The $299 is a floor, not a bill: Katana's own price calculator, embedded on the pricing page, charges per delivered sales order on a tapering rate — $0.1440 per order at 500 a month ($72), $0.1040 at 2,000 ($208) and $0.0594 at 8,000 ($475) — and Core includes one inventory location, with extras at $49 each for a single extra, $42 each at two and $31 each at five. What is the catch with Katana MRP?

The $299 headline is the floor. MigrationPro

Is MigrationPro worth it?

MigrationPro is genuinely best for merchants moving an existing store into Shopify who want products, customers, orders and — critically — URLs and 301 redirects preserved, who are willing to pay the $29 add-on that carries those redirects on the self-service route, and who would rather pay once than subscribe to a tool they will use for a week. Skip it if you are migrating away from Shopify or between two other platforms, since this only imports into Shopify; or you want one firm number before you start, because the price still depends on your record counts and on which of the priced add-ons your source platform turns out to need.

How much does MigrationPro cost?

(re-read 2026-09-18 on apps.shopify.com/migrationpro-shopify-migration-app and migrationpro.io/pricing/) The App Store listing still prints no number at all: one plan, free to install, with the line that pricing is tailored to the entities you migrate and no subscription fees, and a note that all charges are billed in USD. The numbers live on the vendor's own pricing page, which quotes on your product, customer and order counts. Two routes are sold there, both one-time payments rather than subscriptions. The Automated app plan, where you run the migration yourself, starts at $29 based on store size. The Done-for-you plan, where their team handles mapping, testing and go-live with a dedicated specialist, starts at $300 with every add-on included. Both carry a 30-day guarantee, need no credit card, and begin with a free demo of up to 10 products, customers and orders. What has changed since 23 August is the add-on table: each one now carries a price. 301 redirects, order ID preservation, categories mapped to automated collections and content images are $29 each; customer groups to tags and default stock quantity are $19 each; spam customer detection, active products only and tax-inclusive pricing are $39 each; merging short and long descriptions, generating variants from options and resetting the target store are free. The homepage puts a typical store at $49 to $299 on actual record counts, and the FAQ says most stores finish in one to three hours, with 10,000+ products taking longer but still completing the same day. The app is English-only, migrates from WooCommerce, Wix, Squarespace, BigCommerce, Magento, PrestaShop, OpenCart, Shopware, Etsy, Amazon, CSV and Excel among others, and covers products, customers, orders, collections, images, URLs and 301 redirects, with Smart Sync to re-import data changed during the run. Developer Migrationpro, LLC, Dover, Delaware; the app launched 10 May 2022 and now carries 186 reviews averaging 5.0 — 181 five-star, 4 four-star and, for the first time in our record, one at a single star. Note that migrationpro.io/pricing without the trailing slash now answers 308. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with MigrationPro?

The listing tells you nothing about what this costs. “Free to install. Additional charges may apply”, and a single plan block saying pricing is tailored to the entities you migrate. To learn a number you have to leave the App Store for the vendor's calculator, and the first number you find there — $29 — is the floor for the smallest store on the self-service route, not the price for yours. Their own homepage says a typical store lands between $49 and $299. Run the calculator before you form an opinion about value. The add-on structure used to be the unknowable part, and as of 18 September 2026 it is not: the pricing page lists every add-on with a price, from $19 to $39, and marks all of them included on the managed plan. That is a real improvement in what a buyer can read before committing. It also makes the self-service route look less like $29 — the 301 redirects add-on, which is the one thing a migrating store can least afford to get wrong, is a $29 extra on a $29 migration. The marketing still runs ahead of the evidence. The pricing page says “Trusted by 250,000+ stores”, the homepage uses the same figure for migrations, and the FAQ says 250,000+ migrations over 13 years — one number doing three different jobs. The same page claims 5.0 from 170+ verified App Store reviews while the listing itself shows 186, of which 181 are five-star, 4 are four-star and one is a single star. The reviews are genuinely good; weigh them rather than the banner. One practical limit sits in the reviews rather than the marketing. The FAQ says most stores finish in one to three hours and that 10,000+ products may take longer but still complete the same day, while a merchant migrating a very large catalogue in July 2026 wrote plainly that large volumes take a very long time and that the team helped speed it up. If you are moving tens of thousands of records, let the free demo and your own record count decide which of those two you are, not the FAQ.

MindStudio

Is MindStudio worth it?

MindStudio is genuinely best for operators and teams that want to build and deploy real AI agents and workflows visually, across many models, without engineering time. Skip it if you need one simple chatbot (simpler tools exist) or deeply custom logic that ends up easier in actual code.

How much does MindStudio cost?

(prices re-verified 2026-09-18 on mindstudio.ai/pricing) The subscription is a floor, not the bill: every tier reads “+ usage”, so model calls are charged on top. Free is $0/month plus usage and caps you at one agent and 1,000 runs a month. Individual is $20/month plus usage, or $16/month plus usage billed yearly, a stated 20% saving, and lifts both caps to unlimited agents and unlimited runs. Business is quote-based. Because the metered part is uncapped by the plan, the tier you pick says little about what you will actually pay — the run volume does. The metered half is more governable than that makes it sound, and the page is specific about it. A run is one execution of an agent, triggered manually or automatically. MindStudio states it does not mark up model cost — its FAQ says you pay exactly the same price as bringing your own API keys from the providers — and that you can set per-agent and total spend limits and track run cost per agent. All tiers reach 200+ models through its Service Router without API keys of your own, with bring-your-own keys as an option, and hitting a run limit triggers a notification with the choice of upgrading or buying additional runs rather than a hard stop mid-month. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with MindStudio?

The no-markup model pass-through is genuinely fair — but it also means your real cost is subscription PLUS model spend, and an agent that loops or processes big documents burns provider credits invisibly until you set limits. The honest build discipline: start every agent with usage caps, and treat 'no-code' as no-code for the plumbing — the logic design and prompt quality are still entirely on you.

Miro

Is Miro worth it?

Miro is genuinely best for teams that think visually — brainstorms, flowcharts, retros, workshops and planning on an infinite shared canvas with strong templates and facilitation tools. Skip it if your work is mostly documents or task tracking (a doc or PM tool fits better) or most of your company would only ever need to view, not edit.

How much does Miro cost?

(price re-verified 2026-09-02 on miro.com/pricing/) Free plan: 3 editable boards workspace-wide, unlimited members, AI published as a limited trial rather than a credit count. Starter $8/member/mo and Business $20/member/mo, both billed yearly; Enterprise custom priced from 30 members. The yearly toggle is marked save 20%, which puts the monthly rates at $10 and $25. AI credits are published per plan: 25 per member per month on Starter, 50 on Business. MCP calls are a separate published meter: 500 a day on Starter, 2,000 on Business. Currency note: the served page defaults to USD, but rendered from a Dutch connection the same numerals appear as €8 and €20, so the symbol follows the visitor region. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Miro?

Per-member pricing charges for every member added to the workspace, so an org-wide rollout multiplies at full headcount — and the free plan's 3-board cap is workspace-wide, not per person, so real teams outgrow it in days. Model who truly needs to edit before you buy seats.

Modge

Is Modge worth it?

Modge is genuinely best for desk-bound remote or hybrid teams of a few to a few hundred people who want time tracking, app classification, shift adherence, leave and payroll-ready timesheets in one seat priced between $4 and $7 a month, and who are happy to run screenshots as an opt-in rather than a default. Skip it if you need SSO or a SOC 2 report today, both are marked soon; your people work in the field, since the agent is desktop-only with no mobile or GPS on the page; or your security team will not accept a compliance claim without a document.

How much does Modge cost?

(read 2026-09-03 on modge.com/pricing, both billing toggles and the comparison table) Per-seat plans with a full-feature 14-day trial and no card. Basic is $5 per seat per month billed monthly or $4 billed annually: app and URL tracking with AI productivity classification, workforce dashboards, automatic time tracking with optional screenshots, timesheets, approvals and payroll exports, invoice creation, email and chat support. Pro is $7 monthly or $5.50 annually and adds shifts, scheduling and adherence, leave, breaks and overtime, client billing through Stripe, on-demand screenshots with 12-month retention, and custom roles, teams and project budgets. Premium, marked popular, is $9 monthly or $7 annually and adds automated project and task time tracking, workflow analysis, suspicious-activity and mouse-jiggler detection, burnout-risk and profitability insights, contract signing, 24-month screenshot retention and a dedicated account manager. Enterprise is custom, on annual billing with volume pricing, custom retention, customised onboarding and an SLA. Two add-ons are priced per seat: More Screenshots at $2 a month lifts capture from 18 to 60 an hour on Basic, Pro and Premium; Extended Data Retention at $2 a month or $1.67 annually keeps records for six years on every tier. The comparison table lists screenshot retention as 3 months on Basic, 12 on Pro and 24 on Premium, and marks SSO (SAML 2.0), SOC 2 Type II and ISO 9001 as soon. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Modge?

The numbers on Modge's own pages do not agree with each other. The homepage says trusted by 1,000+ teams, the affiliate page says 1,800+ teams, the pricing page says over 2,000 users; the pricing page also shows three star ratings, 4.6 from 1,500 reviews, 4.8 from 2,000 and 4.2 from 800, without naming a single platform they come from. None of that touches the product, but it is the kind of copy a buyer should not repeat in a business case. What does touch the product is the comparison table: SSO through SAML 2.0, SOC 2 Type II and ISO 9001 are all marked soon, and HIPAA compliant appears with no report behind it. If your security review needs a document today, there is none to hand over. The desktop agent is macOS and Windows only, and the pricing page mentions no mobile app or GPS, so field teams are out of scope.

Momence

Is Momence worth it?

Momence is genuinely best for class-based businesses — studios, gyms, salons, schools — that want scheduling, memberships, payments and marketing in one system with a member app. Skip it if you only need a simple calendar-booking link (a lightweight scheduler is cheaper) or you won't accept platform fees on top of card processing.

How much does Momence cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-06 on the vendor's own pricing page) The tiers render in plain text again: Basic $0/mo with a 5% Momence fee on every transaction plus 4% passed to the client; Pro $60/mo with your fee halved to 2.5%; Custom $199/mo with no Momence fee, unlimited add-ons and dedicated support. Basic and Pro carry an identical exclusion list in their card tooltips — no add-ons, no phone number, no inbox, no retail products, no reporting or insights, no staff members, no locations — so Pro buys a lower commission and nothing else. Card processing is charged separately and published per country: US 3.9% + $0.30 online, 3.7% + $0.05 in person, 1.8% ACH; UK 2.5% + 20p, 2.4% + 10p, 2% + 20p BACS; Australia 2.75% + A$0.30, 2.75% + A$0.10, 2% + A$0.30 BECS; Canada 3.9% + C$0.30, 3.7% + C$0.05, 2% + C$0.40. One change since our August readings: on 15 and 19 August 2026 these cards were rendered and then covered by a lead form and a blur filter, so the figures existed in the page but were unreadable until you handed over your details. That gate is gone. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Momence?

The 'free' plan is the most expensive one once you have real volume: a 5% commission on every sale overtakes Pro's $60 flat fee around $29k a year in processed revenue, and Pro in turn loses to the $199 Custom plan above about $67k a year. Pick your tier by volume, not by sticker — and note what the middle tier does not include. The tooltip on the Pro card lists the same exclusions as the free plan: no add-ons, no phone number, no inbox, no retail products, no reporting or insights, no staff members, no locations. Pro halves your commission and adds no features.

monday.com

Is monday.com worth it?

monday.com is genuinely best for teams who want one visual system for projects and work, and who will actually use the automations and views that justify the per-seat price at Standard or above. Skip it if you are two or three people who need a shared list. The free tier caps at 2 seats and 3 boards, and paying per seat for views you will not use is the most common way this gets expensive. Also skip it if you only want the CRM, since that is a separate subscription and worth pricing against dedicated CRMs.

How much does monday.com cost?

(read 2026-08-26 on monday.com/pricing over a US connection, at their default of 10 seats on yearly billing) Free covers two seats. Basic is $9 per seat a month, a total of $90; Standard $12, down from $14, for $120 instead of $140; Pro $19, down from $22, for $190 instead of $220. Enterprise is quoted. The seat count is the thing to watch: the per-seat figure moves with team size, so a price read at ten seats is not what three or fifty will cost. Yearly billing is marked as 18% off, and each paid tier carries its own monthly AI-credit allowance rather than selling AI separately. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with monday.com?

The seat price is not the product price. monday sells four products — Work Management, CRM, Dev and Service — each on its own subscription, and the $9 you see first is the cheapest tier of one of them. A sales team that wants the CRM is buying a second subscription on top of whatever Work Management costs, with a three-seat minimum of its own. The second number to find before you commit is the automation cap. Standard allows 250 automation actions a month across the whole account. If you are buying monday because of the automations you saw demoed, 250 is roughly one workflow running a few times a day — and the next step up is Pro at $19 a seat against Standard's $12. Free is capped at two seats and three boards, which makes it a preview rather than a plan.

Motive

Is Motive worth it?

Motive is genuinely best for fleets large enough that ELD compliance and dash-cam evidence are already costing you money, and who will negotiate a quote rather than accept the first one. Skip it if you run a handful of vehicles and want to know the price before a sales call — that is not available here, and competitors in this category do publish per-vehicle rates.

How much does Motive cost?

(2026-08-30, read on their own pricing page) No prices are shown. gomotive.com/pricing resolves to a lead-capture form at /get-in-touch/?id=pricing — a four-step email capture before any figure appears, preceded by a geolocation prompt offering the UK site. No per-vehicle rate, no plan names with amounts, no contract length and no hardware cost is published on the pricing page, the Pro plan page or the products overview. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Motive?

A pricing page with a form where the prices should be. That is the finding, and it matters more in fleet than in software generally. The real cost here is not a monthly rate: it is hardware per vehicle, a contract term that is usually multi-year, and an early-termination position you will not hear about until you want out. None of that is public. Before the demo call, decide your own numbers: vehicles, contract length you will accept, and what you currently pay per truck. In a quote-only category the first number you hear is anchored to what they think you can pay, not to a rate card.

MRPeasy

Is MRPeasy worth it?

MRPeasy is genuinely best for small manufacturers, wholesalers and distributors who want dependable production planning and inventory control without a heavy ERP or a dedicated IT team. Skip it if you're a large or highly complex manufacturer, or you need deep customisation, advanced analytics and forecasting — MRPeasy is built for simplicity, not complexity.

How much does MRPeasy cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-24 on their own pricing page) Four tiers, per user per month: Starter $49, Professional $69, Enterprise $99 and Unlimited $149. Only Unlimited carries a two-user minimum. We had that minimum down as applying to every tier; MRPeasy's partnerships team wrote on 24 August 2026 that it applies to Unlimited alone, and their pricing page prints minimum 2 users under that one plan and nowhere else. Those four rates are what you pay month to month. Pay for a year and one month is free, which the vendor works out as effective rates of Starter $44.92, Professional $63.25, Enterprise $90.75 and Unlimited $136.58; the monthly and annual toggle did not change a single figure on the page when we clicked it in either direction, so those four are the vendor's arithmetic and not our own reading. Mind the currency, because it is not a conversion: the same pricing page served our European browser 39, 59, 79 and 125 euro per user per month on 24 August 2026, with the user bundle at 69 euro, which is about a fifth under the dollar list. Check what your own checkout quotes before you budget. All plans include unlimited orders and items. The scaling rule is unusual and worth understanding. The first 10 users pay the tier rate, and from the 11th user you add flat $79 bundles of 10 users. So 11 to 20 users is the base price plus $79, 21 to 30 is plus $158, 31 to 40 plus $237, and so on. Expert support is a separate purchase: one hour a month at $199 billed quarterly ($229 for a single month), two hours at $299 ($349 single) and four hours at $499 ($579 single), with training at $150 an hour. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with MRPeasy?

Paying ~$50 per user just so a shop-floor worker can clock in and out feels steep when they'll touch a fraction of the features. Customisation and reporting are limited, and there's no advanced demand forecasting or AI — so larger or highly complex manufacturers will outgrow it.

Is Murf worth it?

Murf is genuinely best for creators, trainers and product teams that need studio-quality voiceovers in many languages without booking voice actors — explainers, courses, product videos. Skip it if you need real emotional acting range for premium brand work (humans still win), or your volume is tiny enough that free TTS built into your video tool suffices.

How much does Murf cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-14 in a browser; the page renders client-side and opens on annual billing, so check which column you are reading) Studio plans: Free $0 for 10 projects, 10 minutes of voice generation and one editor, with no downloads and no commercial rights. Creator $19/mo billed annually ($228 a year): 100 projects, 24 hours of voice generation a year, 1 editor, all 200+ voices, unlimited downloads and commercial rights. Business $66/mo billed annually ($792 a year): 500 projects, 96 hours of generation a year, business licence, PowerPoint integration and audio-to-text. Enterprise is custom and is where voice cloning, SSO and AI translation sit. Annual billing is advertised as saving 33%. Dub and the API are separate products with their own pricing — Murf's own homepage advertises API voice generation from $0.01 a minute, which is a different meter from the Studio hour allowances above. The Studio ladder on the page is Free, Creator, Business and Enterprise; there is no Growth plan, and Creator and Business are capped at one editor seat each, so a second person means Enterprise. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Murf?

The meter is generation hours per YEAR, not per month — 24 hours sounds generous until you count retakes, and regenerating a script three times bills three times. Voice quality is genuinely strong, but the honest test for any TTS is your specific language, names and jargon: generate your hardest paragraph in the trial before subscribing, because demo voices always sound better than your edge cases.

Mycroft

Is Mycroft worth it?

Mycroft is genuinely best for companies chasing an attestation they cannot staff for — CMMC or FedRAMP to keep bidding on defence and public-sector work, SOC 2 or ISO 27001 to get through enterprise procurement — that would rather buy the platform, the security stack and the people who operate it on one contract than assemble a GRC tool, a pen tester, a vCISO and a monitoring vendor separately and hold them together themselves. Skip it if you already have a security team and only need the evidence-collection and control-monitoring layer, because the managed half is most of what you would be paying for; or you need to compare cost before capability, because nothing here is priced anywhere and every tier ends in a sales conversation.

How much does Mycroft cost?

(read 4 September 2026 on www.mycroft.io/pricing) Mycroft has no public pricing: three packages, and not one number between them. Platform is the entry tier and the only one whose button says Get started rather than something sales-shaped; it bundles the full GRC platform for SOC 2, ISO 27001, GDPR, HIPAA and more, a Cloud Native Application Protection Platform, security, AI and privacy awareness training, 24/7/365 threat intelligence and monitoring, a Trust Center, and what Mycroft calls basic Agentic AI workflows. Scale says Book a demo and adds penetration testing, continuous third-party risk management, a customer success manager and expanded AI workflows. Managed says Talk to sales and adds customised frameworks, customised AI workflows and a dedicated CISO who doubles as your success manager. What the page never does is price any of it: no monthly figure, no per-seat rate, no starting-from, no annual floor, not even for the tier you are invited to start by yourself. We looked past the pricing page before writing that down, because the claim is usually wrong. The FAQ page, the product pages, the CMMC-as-a-Service page and the machine-readable llms.txt all describe the packages in detail and none of them names an amount; the sitemap holds no second pricing page; and the raw HTML of /pricing carries no hidden figure either. So the tiers tell you exactly what you get and leave what you pay entirely to a call. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Mycroft?

The pricing page is a feature list with the prices taken out, and that is a choice rather than an oversight — Mycroft publishes what is in each package down to the individual capability, then stops short of the one line you came for. It matters more here than it would elsewhere, because what Mycroft sells is not a seat of software: it is a platform bundled with a managed service, secure enclaves, infrastructure and identity management, an audit coordinated on your behalf and, at the top, a dedicated CISO. That is a services contract, and services contracts scope on your environment, so the number genuinely will differ per company. It also means you cannot sanity-check the cost against doing it yourself until you have sat through a demo. The second thing to weigh is how much of the evidence is the vendor talking. What you can verify from outside is a handful of named case studies — SmashSend, Weave, Wisedocs, Unified — and the quotes inside them; there is no published customer count and no independent review base to read. The team is not thin: the CEO holds a CISA, the engineering co-founder a CISSP, and the product co-founder co-founded PartnerStack. But a young company promising CMMC and FedRAMP outcomes is asking for trust on a timeline where being wrong is expensive. Ask what happens if the assessment does not pass, and ask it before the second call.

Navan

Is Navan worth it?

Navan is genuinely best for companies that want self-serve travel booking with built-in policy control — genuinely free — and optionally unified expense management on top. Skip it if your travel volume is a few trips a year (any booking site does), or you need expense-only tooling without the travel layer.

How much does Navan cost?

Navan publishes no price list, re-verified 2026-08-17: the pricing URL is a marketing page and neither our July nor our August 2026 capture retrieved an amount. What we can state from our dated reading is the shape. Business Travel is genuinely free to the customer, funded by travel-supplier commissions, and includes booking, policy enforcement and basic expense capture. The Expense product is free for the first 15 users and then charged per user per month, which our reading put at around $15. Enterprise is custom for larger organisations. Because the travel side is commission-funded, the question to ask is not what it costs but what it steers — confirm that your negotiated rates and preferred suppliers survive the policy engine. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Navan?

'Free' is real but has a mechanism: Navan earns commission on bookings made through its inventory, so the platform's incentive is that your travel flows through Navan — pricing on specific routes/hotels deserves an occasional spot-check against direct booking. The paid expense tier is where unified T&E gets real; the 15-free-users threshold makes the trial honest for small teams.
