TextExpander

Is TextExpander worth it?

TextExpander is genuinely best for support, clinical-admin and recruiting teams that need everyone sending identical approved wording — the shared library, permissions, auto-subscribe and SSO are what the paid tiers actually buy, and the SOC 2 and HIPAA-with-BAA posture is the one thing no free expander offers a compliance-bound team. Skip it if you are one person on a Mac with a couple of dozen fixed snippets. macOS Text Replacement is built in and free, Espanso is free and open source across all three desktop platforms, and TextExpander's own free plan now covers 5 active snippets. A subscription only starts earning its keep when you need fill-in fields, scripting, or a second person editing the same library.

How much does TextExpander cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-18 on the vendor's own pricing page; USD per user/month billed annually, excluding tax) The line-up was rebuilt since our July reading and it now starts at free. Free is $0 forever with no credit card, Pro $5 (billed annually at $60), Business $10 (billed annually at $120), Enterprise quoted. Annual billing is sold as two months free against paying monthly; the page does not print the monthly amounts next to the plans, so we do not quote them. The free plan is real but deliberately narrow: 5 active snippets out of an unlimited library, one snippet swap every two weeks, 3 teams, 5 team members and no snippet activity history at all. That history — what lets you see changes and restore deleted snippets — is 24 hours on Pro, 7 days on Business and unlimited only on Enterprise. Team members stay capped at 5 on Free and Pro; unlimited members start on Business, which is also the first tier with single sign-on (Google SSO on Business, all providers plus SCIM on Enterprise). A 30-day trial with full access to every feature and no credit card sits alongside the free plan. The page still carries an important pricing note for international customers, which we could not open on this reading, so treat the listed USD amounts as pre-tax. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with TextExpander?

On a Mac the operating system already does the basic job free: Apple's built-in Text Replacement syncs static snippets across your Apple devices, though Apple's own documentation notes it is not available in every app. And Espanso covers the power-user case free and open source on Mac, Windows and Linux. On Windows there is no built-in equivalent (contrary to a widely repeated claim, PowerToys has no text-expansion utility), so the case for paying is genuinely stronger there. Do not buy it for mobile. The vendor's own mobile page is an early-access signup form for Android and iOS betas, not a shipping product — even though the surrounding copy is written in the present tense. Then add the snippet activity history: nothing at all on the free plan, only 24 hours on the $5 Pro tier, 7 days on Business, and unlimited only on Enterprise. Add the vendor's own note that buyers in VAT/GST countries including the EU, UK and Australia may see an estimated 20% uplift on the listed USD price (read 21 July 2026). For one person with a few dozen fixed snippets, the honest answer is usually don't.

TheTop

Is TheTop worth it?

TheTop is genuinely best for enterprises running AI across several departments, models and applications that cannot answer what the bill bought — organisations with a real AI invoice to explain, a CFO asking for return per team, and a security function that wants oversight without reading employees' conversations. Skip it if you are one person or a small team looking for an assistant: this is no longer that product. The $8 morning-brief tool this page used to describe has been withdrawn, there is no self-serve plan, no published price and no signup — the only route in is a sales call about your existing AI estate.

How much does TheTop cost?

(read 19 September 2026) TheTop publishes no price. The navigation carries no pricing link, every button is Book a demo, and the page this review's figures came from is gone: thetop.com/pricing returns a hard 404, as do /pricing/ and /plans. The $8 a month and $80 a year this review used to quote were withdrawn along with the product they belonged to, and cannot be bought today. Until September this was a personal assistant on a single tier, built around a morning brief from Gmail and a calendar. TheTop is now enterprise software sold through a sales call. Read the figures on their site carefully, because it is full of dollar amounts that are not prices: the dashboard mock-ups show an AI invoice of $196,090, a month billed at $137,644 against a $176,920 baseline, and $39,276 saved. Those illustrate what the product reports, not what it costs. Nothing on the public site says what you would pay for TheTop itself. What the site does commit to about commercial shape: deployment is single-tenant, one dedicated instance per customer; onboarding runs in three steps that start read-only (connect analytics, watch for about a month to establish your number, then point traffic at the Engine); and no traffic migration is needed on day one. Expect a contract quoted against your own AI spend rather than a list price — ask for the pricing basis in writing before the demo, because a layer that bills against measured savings and one that bills a flat platform fee are very different deals. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with TheTop?

The price is the knock, because there isn't one. TheTop publishes no rate of any kind: no list price, no per-seat figure, no percentage, and no pricing page — we checked on 19 September 2026 and the old one returns a 404. Everything runs through Book a demo, so the first number you hear will be quoted to you, with your own AI spend already on the table. That is a weak position to negotiate from, and it is worth asking for the basis (flat platform fee, per request, or a share of measured savings) before you show anyone your invoice. The savings claim is measured by the vendor, against a baseline the vendor computes. The site says eligible traffic is priced against your own baseline and shows 22% and 43% saved in its examples, but the party doing the measuring is also the party being paid. Ask how baseline is defined, which traffic counts as eligible, and whether you can audit the calculation — a reuse or reroute that quietly swaps a frontier model for a cheaper one also changes the output, and only your own evaluation will tell you whether that trade was worth it. One more thing to weigh: this product sits in the request path. The Engine applies memory, caching, routing and policy before traffic reaches the model, which is exactly where the savings come from and also exactly where an outage or a bad routing rule lands on every AI feature you run. The vendor's answer is that you start read-only and move traffic when you choose, which is the right shape — but the day you point production at it, a middle layer has become a dependency.

Thryv

Is Thryv worth it?

Thryv is genuinely best for small service businesses — trades, salons, clinics — that currently run on a diary, a spreadsheet and a separate invoicing tool, and would genuinely retire all three. Skip it if you already have a CRM you like, because this is an all-in-one and its value is the bundle; buying it alongside what you have is paying twice for the overlap.

How much does Thryv cost?

(read 2026-09-19 on thryv.com/pricing in a real browser with the billing toggle switched both ways; the page refuses plain HTTP clients with a 403) Two published tiers and a quote-only third. The toggle opens on Monthly, where Starter is $99 a month and Signature $399. Switching to Annual, marked 'Save 15%', takes them to $85 and $340 — both still printed per month. The page prints no yearly total and does not say whether the annual term is charged up front, so any four-figure annual figure, including the $4,068 this review used to quote, is arithmetic rather than a published price. The third tier is Custom, 'Tailored pricing and solutions', sold through Talk to Sales; the Amplify name this dossier carried is no longer on the page. Both paid tiers offer a free trial. Alongside the plans sit four done-for-you services — Ads, Brand, Search and Social Boosts — none of them priced on the page, and the FAQ states they can be added to Signature and Custom plans only, not to Starter. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Thryv?

Two published tiers, $85 and $340, and nothing between them. That makes the decision unusually blunt: not which plan fits, but whether a fourfold step is worth taking all at once. And price the commitment from what the page actually says, which is less than you would like — it prints both annual figures per month, states only that the year saves 15%, and never says whether the term is charged up front. Before that call, list which of the bundled parts you would otherwise buy separately: Thryv's case rests on replacing four or five tools, and if you only want two of them the arithmetic usually favours keeping them. Worth knowing too that the Boosts services, the part sold hardest, cannot be attached to Starter at all.

What is the catch with Toggl?

How much does Top Echelon cost?

Is Tresorit worth it?

How much does Tresorit cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-14, read on their own business and personal pricing pages) There are three business plans: Professional for 1 user, Business from 3 users and Business Pro from 5 users. No free plan is mentioned. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Turbotic?

It's a newer, enterprise-leaning automation platform, so it's less proven with a smaller community than the big names — and the credit-per-execution model means heavy volume needs cost-checking. Verify it integrates with your actual stack first.

Read the full Turbotic review →

Typewise

Is Typewise worth it?

Typewise is genuinely best for customer-service teams with real ticket volume that want AI agents to resolve requests end to end and act inside the systems they already run — CRM, ERP, billing, commerce — with approval steps and human handover as they raise the automation level. Skip it if your helpdesk's built-in AI answers already absorb the volume, or your channel mix leans on phone support — their home page lists voice among the channels while their own SMB page still marks it "coming soon", so pin that down before you sign.

How much does Typewise cost?

(re-read 2026-09-05 on typewise.app/pricing, a page that did not exist in August) A base plan plus a per-resolution rate. Starter is $99 a month or $999 a year with 1 user, email and chat, a $20 monthly AI Operator budget and pay-as-you-go resolutions at $2 each. Growth is $599 a month or $5,999 a year with 5 users, all digital channels, a $100 AI Operator budget and volume packages that bring resolutions down to $1. Business is $2,000 a month billed annually with unlimited workspaces, a $1,500 AI Operator budget, a custom DPA and a success consultant. Additional users are $89 a month. Every plan starts with a 14-day trial of 100 resolution credits and $10 of AI Operator credit, no card. The page defines the meter: a full resolution counts once, and unresolved tickets are free. Prices are in USD and shown in EUR, GBP or CHF at parity depending on your location. History: in August 2026 the only published number was 'starting from $1 per resolution' on the home page and /pricing returned a 404. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Typewise?

The bill has two parts and both are published: a base fee from $99 and a resolution rate from $2 (pay as you go) down to $1 with volume packages. The meter is published too — a ticket closed end to end counts as one resolution, a hand-off to a person counts as half, and a ticket passed straight through or flagged is free. So a conversation the AI cannot finish still bills, at half rate, which is fair but worth budgeting for. What the page still does not say is whether a reopened ticket counts again; get that in writing before the trial. The ROI calculator on their site is the vendor's own arithmetic; the pilot is where you replace it with yours.

Read the full Typewise review →

UltaHost

Is UltaHost worth it?

UltaHost is genuinely best for budget-conscious owners, developers and small businesses that want cheap, fast NVMe hosting (shared or VPS) with free migration and DDoS protection, and will prepay a multi-year term. Skip it if you run a business-critical, high-traffic site that needs premium managed hosting with guaranteed enterprise support, or you don't want to lock into a multi-year term to get the good rate.

How much does UltaHost cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-09 on ultahost.com/shared-hosting) The term ladder is Monthly, Yearly -30%, 2 Years -40% and 3 Years -50%, and the page opens on the two-year term. At that term the shared plans are Starter $3.44/mo (list $6.89) for 1 domain, ~10,000 visits a month and 30 GB NVMe; Basic $5.18 (list $9.19) for 4 domains and ~15,000 visits; Business $10.35 (list $17.02) for unlimited domains and ~25,000 visits; and Pro $13.23 (list $21.62). Every card now states 'Billed for 24 month term. Renews at [the same rate] /mo for 2 years. Cancel anytime.', which settles the renewal contradiction our August capture recorded. The banner above the table advertises a different entry price, $4.13. 30-day money-back guarantee, free SSL, free backups, cPanel. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with UltaHost?

It's the classic budget-host trade-off, with one thing in its favour. The sticker price is a term commitment, not a monthly rate: the headline figures are the 2-year prices, the discount ladder runs -30% yearly, -40% for two years and -50% for three, and paying monthly gives all of it back. What has changed is the renewal. Each plan card now states it renews at the same rate for another 2 years, so the usual trap of a cheap first term followed by a dear one is at least written down. Read that line on the plan you actually pick, because it is quoted per plan. The headline rates also sit under a running 40%-off sale, so treat the crossed-out price as marketing rather than a baseline. Support is generally fast but can be hit-or-miss depending on which agent picks up your ticket, and it's a smaller, less-established brand than the big managed hosts. It's a value play, not a premium-SLA one.

Read the full UltaHost review →

Unleashed Software

Is Unleashed Software worth it?

Unleashed Software is genuinely best for manufacturers and wholesalers who have outgrown spreadsheets and need real-time stock across multiple warehouses, with batch and serial tracking, landed costs and purchase-to-sale visibility in one system. Skip it if your order volume is low or steady enough that the included 100 a month covers it and a lighter tool would do — at that size you are buying capacity you do not use, and the per-order tiers only pay back once volume is there.

How much does Unleashed Software cost?

(prices read 2026-08-17 on unleashedsoftware.com/pricing) Two bundles, in USD and excluding tax. Unleashed Core is $399 a month, or $4,389 a year which the page itself puts at $366 equivalent monthly. Unleashed Pro is $729 a month, or $8,019 a year at $668 equivalent monthly. The toggle promises “Save up to 10%”; the actual saving works out at 8.3% on Core and 8.4% on Pro. Both bundles come with a 14-day free trial with all features enabled. What the headline covers is the part worth reading twice: Core includes 3 user licences, Pro 5, and both include just 100 sales orders a month and 250,000 or 500,000 API calls. Everything past that is priced separately. Extra users are $69 a month on Core and $89 on Pro. Sales-order upgrades run $70 (up to 500), $200 (up to 1,500), $320 (up to 3,000) or $490 (unlimited) a month. And support is a paid add-on at $99 a month for Standard or $239 for Premier. Onboarding is a one-off fee on top: $449 self-service, $799 standard, $5,549 full. Optional modules start at $119 a month for Advanced Inventory Manager, $262 for Marketing Accelerator and $450 for CRM. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Unleashed Software?

The 100 sales orders a month included on both bundles is the number that decides your bill, not the $399. A wholesaler running 1,500 orders with six users who wants phone support pays $399 for Core, $200 for the order tier, $207 for three extra seats and $99 for Standard support. That is $905 a month, or 2.3 times the headline, before a one-off onboarding fee of at least $449. Support being a paid add-on rather than part of the plan is the detail most comparison tables miss. Price the bundle you will actually be on: count your busiest month's orders, your real seat count, and decide whether you can live without phone support, then compare that total against the alternatives rather than comparing entry prices.

Read the full Unleashed Software review →

VEA

Is VEA worth it?

VEA is genuinely best for hands-on entrepreneurs and small-business owners buried in operational detail who want AI that learns their specific business and helps surface what's actually holding it back — explicitly not enterprise software. Skip it if you run a larger organization with established teams and processes (VEA itself positions against that), or you need a task-specific tool rather than a business-level advisor.

How much does VEA cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-02 on VEA's own pricing page) Four tiers now, per user per month, monthly against annual: $30 / $25, $50 / $40, $150 / $120 and $597 / $497. The three-tier ladder we read in August ($31.95 / $24.95, $49.95 / $39.95, $77.95 / $59.95) was replaced between our 2 August and 1 September 2026 captures: the two lower tiers were rounded to whole dollars, the old top tier at $77.95 is gone, the new third tier costs roughly double it, and a fourth tier sits above that. The page's marketing line still says 'Starting at $24.95 per user per month', a figure no longer on the plan cards. Annual billing saves roughly 17% to 20%. The tiers are separated by how much the AI does rather than by seats: the entry plan is task tracking and team alignment, First Class adds AI agents handling routine work, and Private Jet adds AI analysis and decision guidance. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with VEA?

The pitch is unusually bold: an AI that learns your business the way a great employee would, finds the constraint keeping you stuck, and helps you solve it. That ambition is also the honest caveat — it's a young product making a big promise, its price ladder was rebuilt in August 2026 with a top tier at $597 per user a month, and independent review coverage is still thin. Judge it in the trial on one measurable question: did it identify a constraint you recognize as painfully real? If yes, it earns the next month. If its advice stays generic — the thing its own positioning promises to avoid — walk.

Velora

Is Velora worth it?

Velora is genuinely best for nonprofits that want donor CRM, fund accounting and online fundraising from one vendor, purpose-built for charities rather than adapted business tools. Skip it if you're a tiny charity that only needs a donation page (free tools cover that), or you're deeply invested in one best-of-breed piece already.

How much does Velora cost?

Quote-only. joinvelora.com (read in a browser on 5 September 2026) has no pricing page and no amount anywhere; every call to action is 'Talk to sales'. Velora bundles Keela (CRM), Aplos (accounting) and Raisely (fundraising) for nonprofits, and the individual product prices we recorded earlier (Keela from about $134 a month, Aplos from about $79) are no longer shown on the unified site, so treat them as historical until sales confirms. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Velora?

Velora is three acquired products (Keela, Aplos, Raisely) being unified into one platform, and that's the honest watch-out: integration between them is newer than the marketing suggests, so check how deeply the pieces actually connect for your workflow before committing. Costs also stack per product and per contact count — a growing donor base raises the CRM bill. For nonprofits though, purpose-built beats adapted business software in fund accounting specifically.

WebCatalog

Is WebCatalog worth it?

WebCatalog is genuinely best for people juggling many web apps and multiple accounts per service who want them as clean, separate desktop apps with per-profile isolation. Skip it if browser tab discipline or built-in PWA installs already cover you, or you use a handful of apps with single accounts.

How much does WebCatalog cost?

(read 2026-08-25 on their own pricing page, re-read 2026-09-10 in a browser) Basic is free and no card is required: 2 desktop apps, 2 profiles and 1 space. Pro is $5 per user per month billed annually, plus local taxes, and lifts those caps to unlimited apps, profiles and spaces with cloud sync, pages, theme colours, an ads and tracker blocker, an app lock and location services. Business is $8 per user per month billed annually and adds shared spaces, shared pages, team management, centralised billing and an activity log, with SAML single sign-on. Both paid plans carry a 7-day trial. The page prices in USD, EUR or JPY. A legacy lifetime licence is not mentioned on the pricing page or in the help docs (checked 2026-09-10). Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with WebCatalog?

It solves a real annoyance: dozens of web apps and multiple accounts per app as tidy desktop windows. The honest question is whether you need the paid tier at all. The free plan's 2-app limit is tight, but browser profiles and PWAs solve part of the same problem for free. Pro earns its fee when multi-account juggling (several Gmail/Slack/WhatsApp identities) is daily reality.

Read the full WebCatalog review →

Wing Assistant

Is Wing Assistant worth it?

Wing Assistant is genuinely best for founders drowning in delegatable admin who have processes to hand off and want a managed VA without recruiting one themselves. Skip it if your workflows live only in your head — document or automate the repetitive work first, before paying a human to run undefined chaos.

How much does Wing Assistant cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-17 against Wing's own machine-readable pricing page) This is a managed assistant service, not software. General Virtual Assistant part-time is $699 a month and full-time $999 a month, which Wing presents as a 16% saving against the part-time rate pro-rated. Specialist roles are priced separately and enterprise is custom. The published package includes a dedicated assistant, a success manager and free replacement if the fit is wrong. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Wing Assistant?

You're renting a person, not automating a task — a good VA is real leverage, but only if you already have documented, delegatable work. Hand over chaos and you'll get chaos back, faster.

Read the full Wing Assistant review →

World Businesses for Sale

Is World Businesses for Sale worth it?

World Businesses for Sale is genuinely best for owners selling established, often brick-and-mortar businesses — restaurants, shops, services, small manufacturers — who want international buyer exposure with hands-on guidance. Skip it if you're selling a small online asset (an auction-style online-business marketplace fits better), or you want a free listing — even the cheapest no-commission plan here costs £49 for 30 days.

How much does World Businesses for Sale cost?

(2026, re-verified 2026-09-10 on their own selling pages) Browsing is free for buyers. Sellers pick one of two tracks. Broker plans: Basic £395 (3% commission, 6-month contract), Standard £749 (2% commission, 9 months), Premium £1,500 (1% commission, 12-month contract, stays live until sold with free renewals) — every tier states commission is due only if they introduce the buyer. Or no-commission pay-as-you-go listings (inc. VAT): Basic £49, Standard £75 or Premium £99 for 30-day access. Take 10% off on a flex monthly subscription (£44.10 / £67.50 / £89.10) or 20% off on a 6-month plan (£235.20 / £360.00 / £475.20), and you keep 100% of the sale price. Free 24/7 chat guidance before you commit. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with World Businesses for Sale?

On the broker plans you pay twice: a listing fee up front AND commission when it sells — 3% on Basic, 2% on Standard, 1% on Premium — on contracts of 6, 9 and 12 months. Only Premium stays live until sold with free renewals, so on the lower tiers a business that has not sold needs a paid renewal, and for a slow sale £1,500 once can beat £749 twice. Every tier says commission is payable only when they introduce the buyer, which is exactly the clause to read in the contract before you sign. If you want no commission at all, the pay-as-you-go plans start at £49 for 30 days — far cheaper up front, but those are listings, not broker introductions. As with any marketplace, the fee buys exposure, not a guaranteed sale.

Read the full World Businesses for Sale review →

WP Rocket

Is WP Rocket worth it?

WP Rocket is genuinely best for WordPress owners who want near-instant page-speed gains out of the box without touching cache config. Skip it if you're comfortable configuring a free caching plugin like LiteSpeed Cache or W3 Total Cache and enjoy the tinkering.

How much does WP Rocket cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-14 on their own pricing page, which had not moved since our September reading: the same three licences at the same rates, the same 50/100/500 site-count selector on Multi, and all three tiers now carrying Rocket Insights at no extra cost) These are annual licences, and the page prices in the visitor's currency. Read from Europe it sets EUR and shows Single €49.95/yr (1 website), Plus €99.95/yr (3 websites) and Multi €249.95/yr, and the 95 cents matter because an earlier reading rounded all three down to whole euros. Multi carries a site-count selector at 50, 100 or 500 websites, and anything above 500 is a custom licence. The dollar storefront reads $59, $119 and $299 a year for the same three tiers. Our own pricing screenshot on this page is that USD version, captured 12 August 2026, so the figures beside it are the euro ones from the same day. The two are not a contradiction but two storefronts of the same product. All tiers are feature-identical — you pay for the number of sites. There is no trial; the page offers a 14-day refund instead, and the licence auto-renews after a year, switchable off in your account's Billing section. What the page does not say anywhere is what it renews at. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with WP Rocket?

It's a subscription, not a lifetime license — you keep your settings if you stop paying but lose updates and support, and a technically-minded user can get most of the way there with a free caching plugin. Two things to check before you buy on price. The Multi licence is capped now: 50, 100 or 500 websites, with a custom licence above that, so there is no unlimited tier to grow into. And the renewal rate is not published — the page confirms the licence auto-renews after a year but never states at what price, which is exactly where the Trustpilot complaints cluster.

Read the full WP Rocket review →

Wrike

Is Wrike worth it?

Wrike is genuinely best for mid-to-large teams running complex, cross-functional projects that need robust reporting, workflows and resource management. Skip it if you're a small team that just wants a simple kanban board — a lighter tool is faster to adopt and cheaper.

How much does Wrike cost?

(price re-verified 2026-09-05) A permanent free plan at $0 per user, with no user cap named on the plan card; Team $10 per user a month for 2 to 15 users; Business $25 per user a month for 5 to 200 users. Pinnacle and Apex publish no amount at all — both carry only a Contact us button and the line "Connect with us for pricing". Business and every tier above it is sold as an annual subscription only. Seats are bought in blocks: groups of five up to 30 seats, groups of 10 from 30 to 100, groups of 25 above 100. Self-service checkout covers Team and Business up to 15 users with no add-ons; beyond that the purchase goes through a Wrike representative. All prices are listed in US dollars and exclude local taxes. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Wrike?

It's powerful and scales to complex, cross-team portfolios, but that power brings a learning curve and a per-seat cost that climbs — small teams often find it heavier than they need next to a simpler board tool.

Read the full Wrike review →

Xero

Is Xero worth it?

Xero is genuinely best for small businesses whose bookkeeper or accountant already works in Xero — the ecosystem and the bank feeds are the real product, and the monthly price is small against what the reconciliation time costs. Skip it if you invoice a handful of clients and reconcile by hand — the entry tier at $25 after the promo is buying automation you may not need yet, and simpler invoicing tools cost less.

How much does Xero cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-19 on their own US pricing page) Every headline price is still a promotion: 'Get 90% off your plan for your first 6 months when you buy by 30 September 2026'. Early is $2.50 a month for the first six months and then $25. Growing is $5.50 and then $55. Established is $9 and then $90. New on this reading: the same page carries a notice that Xero subscription prices are increasing from 1 October 2026 — the day after the offer closes — and does not print the new rates. Also new: the tier limits. Early is capped at 20 invoices and 5 bills a month, and the footnote states the invoice limit counts both approving and sending, and that transactions initiated by app partners may automatically contribute to it. Multi-currency and project tracking remain on Established only. The offer terms restrict it to new US customers buying their first organisation with code DC90626308US, cover the base subscription only, and auto-renew monthly at the regular price. Optional add-ons: Inventory Plus (no price shown, and excluded on Early) and Xero Payroll powered by Gusto at $36 a month plus $6 per employee or contractor, which the discount does not cover. Standard ACH bill payments are included; fees apply for other methods, same-day ACH and cross-border. Prices are USD and exclude tax; a downgrade is only allowed one month after an upgrade. Xero prices differently by country. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Xero?

Read the second number, not the first. Every plan on that page is 90% off for six months, and the price that governs the rest of your first year is ten times what the button says: $25, $55 and $90. That is not hidden — it is printed right there — but it is printed smaller, and the plan people pick on the strength of $2.50 a month is the one that becomes $25. Then read the notice further down, because it undercuts even that: Xero says subscription prices are increasing from 1 October 2026, the day after the offer closes, without printing the new rates. So month seven costs $25 or more, and the page will not tell you which. The second thing to check is the entry tier's ceiling: Early is 20 invoices and 5 bills a month, the limit counts approving as well as sending, and connected apps can eat into it on your behalf — which makes Growing at $55 the realistic starting price for anyone billing through other software. And check which country's page you are on, because Xero's tiers and prices differ by market.

Read the full Xero review →

Zedonk

Is Zedonk worth it?

Zedonk is genuinely best for small and mid-size fashion brands that need sales, production, inventory and wholesale in one system built around size curves, seasons and linesheets. Skip it if you are not a fashion business — this is deliberately niche — or you want self-serve, monthly-cancellable software rather than an annual, demo-led ERP.

How much does Zedonk cost?

(price re-verified 2026-09-09 against zedonk.co.uk/pricing; unchanged since our August reading) Modular fashion-ERP, priced per building block and billed annually, all listed as 'prices starting from': PLM £166/mo, ERP Essential £166/mo, B2C £250/mo and B2B £332/mo. The two upper cards read 'All ERP Essential Features PLUS' — Shopify integration and barcode generation on B2C, the B2B digital showroom on B2B — so those tiers contain the ERP rather than sitting on top of it. No self-serve signup: every tier goes through a demo, and the FAQ states the displayed plans are 'merely examples' and that a plan can be changed at any time. Onboarding and training are included in every plan; the FAQ puts implementation cost (customisation, data migration, integrations) in a separate figure quoted in a consultation. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Zedonk?

The 'starting from' wording is doing real work: £166/mo is the floor of the cheapest module, billed annually, so a working setup for a brand that sells wholesale quickly stacks toward £332/mo and beyond — about £4,000 a year before add-ons. There is no trial and no public total: you find out your actual price in a demo call. Budget for the stack, not the sticker.

Read the full Zedonk review →

Zeffy

Is Zeffy worth it?

Zeffy is genuinely best for small and mid-size nonprofits that want donations, ticketing, memberships and payments without any fee eating the mission's money. Skip it if your organization objects to donor-facing tip prompts on principle, or you need enterprise CRM-grade donor management built in.

How much does Zeffy cost?

(re-verified 2026-09-09 on zeffy.com/home/free-online-fundraising-platform) Still genuinely $0 for nonprofits: no platform fee, no transaction fee and no card-processing fee on donations, ticketing, shops, raffles, auctions, peer-to-peer or memberships. Zeffy states it covers transaction and credit-card fees itself and earns only from the optional tip donors are offered at checkout. Their own scale claim on that page: 100,000 nonprofits have raised $2B on Zeffy over seven years without paying a fee. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with Zeffy?

The honest scrutiny of 'free': the tip prompt is shown to YOUR donors, with suggested percentages. Most give happily, but some organizations feel it inserts a step between donor and cause — and a donor who tips 10% effectively paid a fee, just voluntarily and not from your share. Compare that trade against the 2-5% the paid platforms take from every donation, and for most small nonprofits the math strongly favors Zeffy.

Read the full Zeffy review →

ZenBusiness

Is ZenBusiness worth it?

ZenBusiness is genuinely best for first-time founders who want the paperwork handled and are willing to pay a yearly fee to keep it handled — the a-la-carte pricing means you can start at the state fee plus a registered agent and add only what you actually need. Skip it if you are comfortable filing with your state yourself and acting as your own registered agent. In that case the state fee is the only unavoidable cost, and the $199/year is buying convenience and a business address rather than anything you cannot do. Also skip it if you need legal advice rather than filing, since a formation service is not a law firm.

How much does ZenBusiness cost?

(re-verified 2026-08-27 on their own pricing page; unchanged since 25 Jul 2026) Formation itself is $0 plus the state's fee — that applies to LLC, S corp and C corp filings alike, and the state fee is separate, varies by state and is not shown until you pick one. Everything else is priced as its own line rather than bundled into plan tiers. The recurring items are registered agent at $199/year, Worry-Free Compliance Filing at $199/year, and Advanced Compliance Protection at $299/year. One-time filings: operating agreement $99, EIN $99, business document templates $99, rush filing $79 and DBA registration $95 plus state fees. Then business licence report $149, amendment $100, certificate of good standing $100, annual report filing $100 per report, and New York publication $200 for the state that requires it. There is a second catalogue alongside the legal one: business website $129/year, eCommerce website $279/year, business email address $25/year, domain $25/year, domain privacy $10/year, logo kit $60, business banking $5/month and Money Pro with online payments $30/month. Always verify the live price.

What is the catch with ZenBusiness?

The $0 is real for the filing service and it is also the whole commercial idea: what you sign up for at zero has an annual carry that is not shown next to it. A registered agent is a legal requirement in every state, and theirs is $199/year — so the realistic first-year cost of 'the $0 LLC' is the state fee plus $199, before compliance filing at another $199/year. Price the second year, not the first. The second thing to hold in view is that the state fee is the largest and most variable number in the whole exercise, and ZenBusiness cannot change it. It is set by your state, ranges widely, and their own page sends you to a lookup rather than quoting it. Anyone comparing formation services on the advertised price alone is comparing the small half of the bill.

Read the full ZenBusiness review →
